# DECISIONS — the greppable index of what was decided and what was measured

⚠ **This file is an INDEX, never a source.**  Every row points at the document
that owns the decision; the row is one line and the reasoning stays where it
was written.  A second copy of a decision is the copy that drifts.

## The code scheme

Four namespaces, one letter each, three digits, **never reused** — the same
convention `PROBLEMS.md` already uses for `@D001`.

| code | is | index | grep |
|---|---|---|---|
| `@X###` | a **design decision** | this file § Decisions | `grep -rn '@X014' docs/ plans/` |
| `@M###` | a **measurement of record** — a number other documents quote | this file § Measurements | `grep -rn '@M001' .` |
| `@D###` | a **dryopea defect** | [`PROBLEMS.md`](../PROBLEMS.md) | `grep -rn '@D002' .` |
| `@P###` | a **loft** issue (upstream) | loft's own tracker | — |

### ⚠⚠ Plan phases are NOT unique, and must be written `<plan>-<phase>`

A bare phase code is ambiguous today and grep cannot resolve it:

| bare code | plans that use it |
|---|---|
| `S0` | **18** (scenario capture) and **22** (the field cache) |
| `C2` | **09** (lattice conversion) and **15** (the carry model) |
| `V0` | **08**, **13** and **19** |
| `R0` | **20** and **21** |

⚠ So cite a phase as **`19-P3`**, **`22-S0`**, **`12-B7`** — plan number, hyphen,
phase.  That form is unique, greppable, and reads the same as the prose already
does (*"plan 12 B7"*).  ⚠ Existing prose that says "plan 12 § B7" is fine and is
not being rewritten; what matters is that a **code written on its own** carries
its plan.

### Categories

Every `@X` carries one, so the index can be filtered:

| category | what it governs |
|---|---|
| `world` | the lattice, terrain, heights, passability |
| `sim` | the tick, movement, damage, waves, cost |
| `render` | cameras, geometry, frames, art |
| `play` | the loop, input, what the player does |
| `run` | the shape of a session and a run |
| `method` | how the project builds and gates things |

---

## Decisions

### render — entity art ([`PARTS.md`](PARTS.md))

| code | decision | owner |
|---|---|---|
| `@X001` | ⚠ **REVISED 2026-08-15** — ~~a dryopea-native part model~~ → **reuse `hex_body`** (published: rigs, revolute joints with limits, pure poses, per-bone OBBs, canonical text) and **enhance it** with an out-of-plan hinge axis.  ⚠ The first draft read `hex_part` (unpublished) and never checked the registry.  ⚠ `hex_part` itself stays out — trigger unchanged | `PARTS.md` § D1 |
| `@X002` | ⚠ **REVISED 2026-08-15** — ~~box/disc primitives~~ → **hex CELLS WITH HEIGHTS**, authored at the part's own scale.  ⚠ The roundness objection dissolves on `@X044`'s corner smoothing; boxes would have forfeited six published libraries | `PARTS.md` § D2 |
| `@X003` | **the tower's socket is the SIMULATION's** — plan `17-T2`'s detachable top already is moros's socket model, so what is drawn asks `TowerState` and never a second flag | `PARTS.md` § D3 |
| `@X004` | a part emits **GEOMETRY**; there is no sprite and no baked scale.  ⚠ Replaced an earlier sprite design — a sprite sheet does not degrade under a free camera, it *lies* | `PARTS.md` § D4 |
| `@X005` | a **PNG is an ARTEFACT**, never a runtime input.  ⚠ `imaging::Pixel` has no alpha, but a captured frame is opaque, so the hole does not bite | `PARTS.md` § D5 |
| `@X006` | **the durable artefact is the SIZE**, and a test compares a part's footprint to the simulation's constant | `PARTS.md` § D6 |
| `@X007` | the **hover unit**: fixed base, four booms, front rotors r 0.36, **rear r 0.52**, canopy on a rear lateral hinge `0 → 0.30` turns | `PARTS.md` § D7 |
| `@X044`-`@X047` | the terrain mesh's construction, copied from moros — corner heights are a MEAN; **one predicate** (`faced_between`) both stops the blend and draws the wall; emit a face only from the side that stands; the mesh tile is not the store tile.  ⚠ **`@X044` and `@X045` are SUPERSEDED for dryopea by `@X072`** — the mean is a no-op here and there is no blend to stop.  ⚠ **`@X046` is BUILT** (plan 25 M1, `ground_mesh.loft::ground_side_faces`), and both halves of its `hh <= nh` fail invisibly, so it is gated as four COUNTS rather than as a picture | [`RENDERER.md`](RENDERER.md) § R3, `plans/25` § M1 |
| `@X048` | ⚠⚠ **a cell is a COLUMN OF LAYERS**, each with an absolute height — that is where vertical detail comes from.  ⚠ Parts get columns now; the TERRAIN keeps one surface until something must walk UNDER something | `PARTS.md` § D2 |
| `@X051` | **BRIDGES span HOLES, not walkable ground** — `DESIGN.md` makes a cavern a *"hole in the surface map, a non-walkable kind the way sea already is"*, so a span is just a hex at height and ONE surface still answers.  ⚠ The layer trigger fires only if a bridge must cross walkable ground; that is a MOVEMENT change (`Hex` → `(Hex, layer)`), not an art one.  ⚠⚠ A span *"cannot be walled and cannot be flanked"* — terrain that invalidates the learned optimum `@M004` teaches | `PARTS.md` § D2 |
| `@X052` | ⚠⚠ **base bridges robots walk UNDER fire `@X048`'s trigger** — two walkable surfaces over one hex, so the flow field's node becomes `(Hex, layer)` and `hex_height` / `can_step` / occupancy / 1 094 tests are all in scope.  ⚠ **Undesigned, and flagged as such by the project owner.**  `DESIGN.md` already names the feature (`cy`-layer decks, second-phase).  ⚠ Three seeds: a tower above its own body ramp, a crew route the wave does not share, somewhere to park that is not in the way | `PARTS.md` § D2 |
| `@X049` | a **PLACEMENT layer** — where another form MAY go (`SOCK`/`FITS`, `hex_fit`'s doorstep).  ⚠ A refusal must NAME its restriction, because an off-grid value is otherwise **silently snapped**.  ⚠ dryopea has one already: the tower socket | `PARTS.md` § D2 |
| `@X050` | **attachment with LIMITS** — `hex_body`'s `Joint {value, lo, hi}` plus `joint_fits` / `joint_offer` (clamped, not refused) / `joint_residual` (how far past), and `rig_admissible` for a whole pose | `PARTS.md` § D2 |
| `@X038`-`@X043` | the build pipeline — a script in, a `.glb` and validation frames out.  ⚠ `mesh3d` + `glb` are published, so the emitter is a MAPPING; the glb is a baked POSE not a rig; validation is measured **and** a cold read | `PARTS.md` § D9 |
| `@X008` | the **crew is the same part** as the player (*"same chassis"*), and the four robot classes are **one part with per-class DATA** | `PARTS.md` § D8 |
| `@X087` | ⚠⚠ **a socket rides a BONE at `t`, a binding carries NEITHER a coordinate NOR a pose, and where a socket IS, is COMPUTED.**  Plan 20 A1's model, `src/part.loft` over `hex_body::Rig`.  ⚠ **The absence is the mechanism** (`hex_part::bind.loft`'s own invariant): move the tower and its top follows, because there is no second coordinate that could be forgotten — so `socket_world` is the ONE re-assertion site and a `bd_q` added later *for convenience* would end the design without failing a test.  ⚠ **Three deviations from `hex_part`'s records, each forced rather than chosen**: a socket is at a bone and a `t` along it because a part here is a `Rig` and not a voxel world (and `t` is the family's own spelling — `hex_fit::feature_fits`); **one class token** rather than a kind AND a size, because dryopea's size is gated against the SIMULATION's constant and a size here would be a second authority on one fact (`@X006`); and **no `bd_open`**, because that field is right in a saved DOCUMENT and wrong in a catalogue asset whose angles come from the sim (`@X003`) — storing one would be exactly the second flag § D3 refuses.  ⚠ **The refusals are dryopea's `*_fault` text rather than `hex_part`'s code-plus-why**, and what survives the change of spelling is `K-FIT`'s real requirement: a refusal NAMES its restriction, so a missing part and a cycle never share a wording.  ⚠⚠ **The cycle check carries a PATH, not a visited set**, and the DIAMOND is what tells them apart — one part in two sockets is legal, and a global *seen* set refuses a catalogue that is fine (`@M036`) | `plans/20` § A1, `part.loft`, `PARTS.md` § D3, `@X001`, `@X003`, `@X006`, `@M036` |
| `@X088` | ⚠⚠ **A PART'S FOOTPRINT IS DERIVED FROM ITS LIMBS, NEVER DECLARED BESIDE THEM — and the vehicle is 2.28 x 2.05 because that is where the ROTORS are.**  Plan 20 A3.  `PARTS.md` § D6 asks that *"a part declares its footprint, and a test compares it to the number the simulation uses"*, and a part that DECLARED its size would agree with itself for ever — so `src/catalogue.loft` declares LIMBS and `part_size` computes the extent.  ⚠⚠ **The size question was the project owner's and it was a SIMULATION change** (2026-08-18): § D7's 2.28 x 2.05 wins over `numbers.json`'s 1.1 x 2.4, which is a different SHAPE rather than a bigger one — a wide short quadcopter against a long narrow car — and the vehicle is now wider than the 1.5 m hex it occupies, with `blocker_at` unchanged and nothing deriving passability from art.  ⚠ The HEIGHT is **0.93** and not § D7's rounded *"~0.9"*, because it is derived too: skid bottom −0.37 to canopy top +0.56.  ⚠ **Three copies and two gates** — the limb table, the `.loft` constant, `numbers.json` — because nothing loads the JSON.  ⚠ Three things the catalogue deliberately does NOT carry: a colour (a class is a row of DATA, so one would force an entry per class, § D8), a limb SOLIDITY (dryopea's hitbox is a HEX, and reading one would be the *derive passability from art* § D6 forbids), and § D7's four BOOMS (they run diagonally and nothing carries a REST ORIENTATION — A2's to solve) | `plans/20` § A3, `catalogue.loft`, `PARTS.md` § D6 / § D7, `@X006`, `@X007`, `@M037` |
| `@X089` | ⚠⚠ **A PART EMITS TRIANGLES AND CONTAINS NO FORWARD KINEMATICS — the pose comes from the LIBRARY, one frame per BONE.**  Plan 20 A2, `src/part_mesh.loft`.  Every vertex is placed by `hex_body::frame_point` over a `rig_world_frame3` held across that bone's limbs; a second implementation of a pose is the one a future reader would reach for and would drift from the one the sockets use (`@X001`).  ⚠⚠ **`rig_world_frame3` EXISTS BECAUSE OF THIS PHASE**: `rig_world_seg3` answers a bone's base and tip, both ON its axis, and a limb is a cloud of points that are not — so the basis it computed and discarded became `hex_body` **0.3.0**, proposed and published upstream rather than re-derived here.  ⚠ A NORMAL is a DIRECTION: posing one as a point adds the bone's translation, which still normalises to something plausible and is wrong everywhere the bone is not at the origin — gated by a limb 13 m out.  ⚠ A box gives each face its OWN four corners (24 vertices, not 8) so the normals stay flat, which is `ground_mesh.loft`'s no-blend argument one layer up.  ⚠ **No colour** (a class is a row of DATA and colour is a UNIFORM, `@X074`) and **no world placement** (that is A5, and the only layer that knows `play_alpha`) | `plans/20` § A2, `part_mesh.loft`, `@X001`, `@X004`, `@M038` |
| `@X090` | ⚠⚠ **WHAT IS DRAWN IS READ FROM THE SIMULATION, AND THE CANOPY READS THE CARGO LEDGER.**  Plan 20 A4, `src/pose.loft`.  § D3's rule — *the sprite follows the simulation, and never a second flag* — applied to all three joints the game has: the tower's top is `tower_has_top` (plan 17 T2), the rotors' rate is `vehicle_speed` (plan 13), and the canopy is **`cargo_carrying`** — open exactly while this hover unit is laden.  ⚠ The canopy is the decision, because nothing forced it: it is the one thing `carry.loft` moves that nothing draws, so an open cockpit makes a laden vehicle legible at a glance, which is `DESIGN.md` § HUD's *diegetic* rule getting something for free.  ⚠ A field on `Vehicle` saying *canopy open* would be the defect the whole design is arranged to make unwritable — two facts that can disagree about one thing.  ⚠⚠ **The simulation answers a TARGET and the SWING is presentation**: `pose_hover_unit` takes the angle as a FLOAT so the renderer can ease it (`fixstep::approach`, the camera's own `k`), because a binary door here would have re-introduced the two-pose canopy § D4 retired one layer down | `plans/20` § A4, `pose.loft`, `PARTS.md` § D3, `@X003`, `@X004`, `@M039` |
| `@X091` | ⚠⚠ **A ROTOR'S BOOST RATE IS DERIVED FROM THE VEHICLE'S OWN SPEED RATIO, AND ITS IDLE RATE IS BOUNDED BY THE FRAME RATE RATHER THAN BY THE FICTION.**  Plan 20 A4.  `rotor_turns_per_second` is `ROTOR_IDLE_TURNS_PER_S x (vehicle_speed(v) / VEHICLE_SPEED_HEX_PER_SECOND)`, so the rotors speed up by exactly as much as the vehicle does and there is no second number that can disagree about what boost IS.  ⚠⚠ Two crossed blades repeat every quarter turn, so the apparent direction REVERSES beyond an eighth of a turn per frame — **3.75 turns/s at 30 fps** — while a believable rotor turns at fifty.  So 1.2 is stylised, deliberately, with the boosted figure at 2.4 and a 1.5x margin that a test pins.  ⚠ The PHASE is `rate x t` read fresh rather than integrated, so a change of rate moves it; integrating would need an accumulator on the `Vehicle`, which is the animation state this plan refuses, and the trigger to add one is *something READS a blade's position* | `plans/20` § A4, `pose.loft`, `PARTS.md` § D7, `@X007`, `@M039` |
| `@X092` | ⚠⚠ **AN ENTITY COLOUR IS DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE THE PALETTE, AND THE GATE ACCOUNTS FOR EVERY NON-PALETTE PIXEL BY NAME.**  Plan 20 A5, `src/entity_view.loft`.  `measure.loft`'s founding rule is that a pixel is an EXACT palette colour or a FAULT; an entity painted in a palette colour would be counted as GROUND, so a robot on grass would be absorbed into a band tens of thousands of pixels wide and every share would still read plausible.  So an entity colour is none of the twelve, it lands in `classify_canvas`'s `unknown` exactly as the background does, and `gl_gate.loft::gl_entity_pixels` sums the ten by name.  ⚠ The claim that buys is TOTAL and exact: **every pixel is a palette colour, an entity colour, or the clear colour, and nothing else** — asserted as `unknown - entity pixels == 0` over a frame-filling world with a roster standing in it.  ⚠ `entity_colours_distinct` is what stops it being a wish, and it fired at once (`@M040`).  ⚠ The alternative — a thirteenth bucket for entities — was refused for `@X077`'s reason one layer on: fold them into the classifier and the ONE thing `unknown` is for stops meaning *fault* | `plans/20` § A5, `entity_view.loft`, `gl_gate.loft`, `@X074`, `@X077`, `@M040` |
| `@X093` | ⚠⚠ **A DRAWN CLASS *IS* THE SIMULATION'S MOVER KIND — THERE IS NO SECOND ENUMERATION.**  Plan 20 A5.  `PARTS.md` § D8 makes a class *a row of DATA (a colour, a scale factor, at most one distinguishing limb)*, which is `CLAUDE.md`'s *ONE AI, per-class DATA* one layer up.  So `entity_colour` is indexed by `passable.loft`'s own discriminants, unmapped — `KIND_VEHICLE` is the player's row at 2, scar and all — and the three rows that are not movers (crew, tower base, tower top) APPEND after the last one, which is that file's own rule.  ⚠ **The consequence is that a new robot class costs a colour and nothing else**: no art-side enumeration to keep in step, and therefore no pair that can disagree about which class a robot is.  ⚠ `entity_class_name` delegates to `enemy_class_name` for every mover row for the same reason | `plans/20` § A5, `entity_view.loft`, `passable.loft`, `PARTS.md` § D8, `@X008` |
| `@X094` | ⚠⚠ **A HOVER UNIT IS DRAWN AT THE HEIGHT IT CAN CLIMB, SO BOOST IS VISIBLE FOR NOTHING.**  Plan 20 A5.  `PROXY_ART.md` gives the vehicle *~0.4 m hover clearance* and `vehicle.loft` gives it `VEHICLE_CLIMB_METRES = 0.4` — the same number twice, because the reason a hover unit crosses a 0.4 m step is that it floats 0.4 m off the ground.  So the renderer reads **`vehicle_climb(v)`** rather than restating the constant: there is no second number to disagree, and a boosting vehicle (climb 3.0 m) is DRAWN 3.0 m up, clearing the wall it is about to clear.  ⚠ That is `DESIGN.md` § HUD's *diegetic* rule getting a second thing for free, after the rotors (`@X091`) — and unlike the rotors it survives a flat unlit colour, because it moves the silhouette rather than turning it (`@M039`).  ⚠ A crew member has no boost door, so it floats the idle figure; `entity_emit_hover` decides on the OWNER id, which is the same integer `CargoLayer` is keyed on | `plans/20` § A5, `entity_view.loft`, `vehicle.loft`, `PROXY_ART.md`, `@X091`, `@M039` |
| `@X095` | ⚠⚠ **THE RENDERER DERIVES ITS OWN INVALIDATION BY DIFFING THE HEIGHT LAYER — THE SIMULATION IS NEVER TOLD ANYBODY IS WATCHING.**  Plan 19 P6, `src/play_view.loft`.  `ground_gl.loft` was written for an EDITOR's ground (paint a hex, re-bake the tiles it reaches); a live session moves the terrain for three other reasons — a body falls, a wall breaks, a heap is cleared — and none of them goes near `paint` from the caller's side.  ⚠ A dirty list on `HeightLayer` was the obvious answer and is refused on three counts: it would leak into `state_diff` (which asks *are two runs in the same state*, and a dirty list is not state), survive an `emit`, and grow for ever in the 1397 headless tests that have no renderer.  ⚠⚠ **So `MeshWatch` keeps a SNAPSHOT and diffs it, which is exact only because of one invariant: *every terrain change a TICK can make moves the HEIGHT LAYER*** — a body raises its hex, a breach raises masonry BEFORE it repaints (`break_structure`'s own order), salvage lowers a pile.  The layer is a handful of entries even on a busy base, so the diff is O(piles).  ⚠ **It is asserted against the simulation and not quoted**: `tests/19_p6` plays a real base and compares every maintained tile with a COLD rebuild, which is `11_f8`'s field-cache shape — and the fixture's own check found the first version measuring a base where only BODIES fell, so the one case the diff cannot see directly (a REPAINT) had never happened. | `plans/19` § P6, `play_view.loft`, `mesh_chunks.loft`, `@X075`, `@M041` |
| `@X096` | ⚠⚠ **THE GROUND TILE IS 8x8, DECIDED ON THE MEASUREMENT PLAN 25 M4 COULD NOT MAKE.**  M4 swept `MESH_CHUNK_SHIFT` and **changed nothing**, deliberately: shrinking the tile buys 8.6x on an edit and costs 12x the draw calls, *"and that half cannot be measured from `loft test`"*.  It named its inheritor — *the phase that wires GL into `play_mode`* — and plan 19 P6 is it.  ⚠⚠ **Measured in a real window: the draw cost does not move at all.**  96 tiles draw in the same 7 ms/frame as 8, and 24 in the same 5 ms as 4 — so the tile size is decided by the EDIT cost alone, where 8x8 is **7 ms against 54 ms** on a small world and **7 ms against 112 ms** on one four times the area.  ⚠ At 32x32 a body falling cost a 3-7 frame hitch that GREW with the map; at 8x8 it is constant at 7 ms, inside a 60 fps frame.  ⚠ The cold bake on entering play mode got FASTER too (218 ms against 259), which is M4's own finding that a big tile at the world's edge walks more hexes that are not drawn.  ⚠⚠ **The cost was two test fixtures that encoded 32**, and both are now derived from `mesh_chunk_span()` and pass at 8, 16 and 32: `25_m2`'s coordinates are a TILE BOUNDARY rather than the number 32, and `25_m4`'s two thresholds (a literal `> 500` hexes' worth, and a `> 10x` sparse/dense ratio) were each a statement about the world they were measured in rather than about the mesher. | `plans/19` § P6, `plans/25` § M4, `mesh_chunks.loft`, `@M028`, `@M041` |
| `@X097` | ⚠⚠ **THE HUD IS ONE NUMBER, AND ITS DIGITS ARE RECTANGLES.**  Plan 19 P7.  Two decisions, and the first is `docs/DESIGN.md` § HUD's rather than this phase's: its entire numeric HUD is *"Wallet (points) — one corner number.  The only number the player must see to make build decisions. […] That's the entire HUD.  No wave-number, no inter-wave countdown, no minimap, no boost cooldown bar."*  So there is no health bar, no tick counter, no enemy count and no wave readout, and **adding one is a design change rather than a feature** — everything else is diegetic and already built (the rotors show boost `@X091`, the canopy shows cargo `@X090`, a tower's top shows whether it has one).  ⚠⚠ **The second is SUPERSEDED in part by `@X268` (BACKLOG B1, 2026-08-27) — `draw_text` under `loft test` was measured WORKING, and the font now ships; what still holds is the anti-aliasing argument (`@M047`), so the digits stay rectangles by choice.**  As written it said: `graphics` publishes `draw_text`, and it is unusable twice over — it rasterises through `#native rasterize_text_into`, which answers *"native function not loaded"* under `loft test`, and it needs a FONT FILE this repo does not have.  **A HUD nothing headless can draw is a HUD no test and no `snap` can see**, so the digits are SEVEN SEGMENTS built from `fill_rect`, which every gate in the repo already reads.  ⚠ `picker.loft` reached the same conclusion in plan 01 and its note is the older half of this one.  ⚠ Right-aligned, because a corner readout that grows leftward never moves the digit the eye is resting on; FLOORED, because a tower costs 100 whole points and rounding 99.6 up would show one the player cannot buy; and 0 for a broke run rather than a minus sign. | `plans/19` § P7, `hud.loft`, `play_view.loft`, `DESIGN.md` § HUD, `@X090`, `@X091`, `@M042` |
| `@X098` | ⚠⚠ **THE WALLET'S NUMBER RAMPS AMBER TO RED, AND IT IS A FUNCTION OF THE INTEGER THE DIGITS SHOW.**  Plan 19 P7, `hud.loft` § the ramp (project owner, 2026-08-18 — `@X097` had left it as an open design question and the answer was *add it*).  ⚠ **LINEAR over `WALLET_STARTING_POINTS`**, which is the one curve that invents no number: the two endpoint colours are the only additions, and the span is a constant the simulation already owns.  ⚠⚠ **The threshold a designer would reach for is refused for now** — *the number goes warm when you can no longer buy the cheapest thing* needs `order_cost_points` (100), which lives in `examples/numbers.json` and in **no `.loft` constant**, because building is not built.  Introducing a simulation constant nobody spends, to colour a HUD, is a constant that drifts; the trigger to add the band is BUILDING.  ⚠⚠ **It reads `hud_wallet_points` and not `wallet_left`, and that is two decisions in one.**  The colour can never disagree with the number, because it IS the number — read the float instead and they are two readings of one wallet that can differ by a rounding, invisibly, both plausible.  And it makes the reachable colours FINITE: exactly 201, so `tests/19_p7` sweeps every one of them against the palette, the entity table and the clear colour rather than sampling.  ⚠ That sweep is what caught `@M043`. | `plans/19` § P7, `hud.loft`, `DESIGN.md` § HUD, `@X097`, `@X092`, `@M043` |

### render — the camera and the pipeline ([`RENDERER.md`](RENDERER.md))

| code | decision | owner |
|---|---|---|
| `@X009` | the camera is **moros's `RenderCamera`**, ported not depended on.  ⚠⚠ `camera_overview` at elevation **89° IS dryopea's editor view**, so it is one camera with two presets | `RENDERER.md` § R1 |
| `@X010` | **geometry is shared, rasterisation is not** — GL for the game, the software rasteriser keeps the editor and its 520 measurements.  ⚠ Trigger to collapse: GL reproduces the top-down view | `RENDERER.md` § R2 |
| `@X011` | the **terrain becomes 3-D**, meshed per dirty chunk.  ⚠ The largest single item; probably its own plan | `RENDERER.md` § R3 |
| `@X012` | the gate chain is **xvfb → GL → `gl_screenshot` → `imaging::png` → exact classification** | `RENDERER.md` § R4, `@M002` |
| `@X013` | the gate renders **FLAT UNLIT** — shading turns one palette colour into a range and `unknown` stops meaning *fault*.  ⚠ Never loosen to nearest-colour | `RENDERER.md` § R4 |
| `@X014` | the game's camera lives on **`PlayState`**, never `EditorState.cam`.  ⚠ **BUILT in plan 21 R2**, one phase after the camera itself — until the ease existed there was nothing for a session to REMEMBER between frames | `RENDERER.md` § Open 4, `@X070` |
| `@X015` | **no indoor camera modes** — moros's SNUG / CUTAWAY / EYES answer an interior dryopea's roofless bases do not have | `RENDERER.md` § R1 |
| `@X065` | ⚠⚠ **the 3-D world frame is RIGHT-HANDED — x EAST, y NORTH, z UP — and `lat_to_world` is the ONE place that negates.**  dryopea's `+y` is SOUTH everywhere else, which is a CANVAS convention; carried into 3-D it is left-handed, `mat4_look_at` builds a right-handed basis, and the product is a MIRROR.  ⚠ The negation cancels `lat_to_metres`', so the camera's world is `hex_grid`'s own frame — the library's is a WORLD frame and dryopea's is a SCREEN frame | `RENDERER.md` § R1, `@M021` |
| `@X066` | **`camera_overview` pins the AZIMUTH as well as the elevation** (270°) — a deviation from moros, which leaves it free because a map view there rotates.  dryopea's editor view does not rotate, so an unpinned overview would reproduce it from one orbit position and be a rotated picture of it from every other | `RENDERER.md` § R1, `@M021` |
| `@X067` | **a follow bearing is `azimuth = facing + 180°`, and it comes from the VELOCITY** — the vehicle stores no facing.  ⚠ moros's `azimuth = 270° − facing_deg` says the same thing in moros's frame and puts the camera **ABEAM** in dryopea's (`@M022`).  ⚠ A parked vehicle has NO bearing, so the camera keeps the one it had — `atan2(0, 0)` is 0.0 and would swing the view east on every key release | `RENDERER.md` § R1 + § Open 1, `@M022` |
| `@X068` | ⚠⚠ **the ease is `1 − e^(−k·dt)`, and moros's `f = k·dt` is REFUSED** — the linear form is frame-rate DEPENDENT, and `play.loft` is built on the opposite property (`19-P0`: *1200 frames of 1/60 s and one frame of 20 s reach the same state*).  A camera that disagreed would make a captured frame a function of the frame rate.  ⚠ It also fixes the camera's step to the FRAME clock — stepped last in `play_step`, never inside `play_advance`'s tick loop, where at 60 fps it would run on one frame in forty | `RENDERER.md` § R2b, `@M023` |
| `@X069` | ⚠⚠ **the ease carries THREE valves — target, azimuth and boom — where moros eases the boom alone**, and the reason is dryopea's own: the vehicle is on a LATTICE, so its position is two integers and moves a whole hex (1.299 m) on the tick it steps.  An un-eased follow camera is not a camera with a rough edge, it is a slide show.  ⚠ `plans/21` § R2 names only the boom; that is the phase under-describing itself | `RENDERER.md` § R2b, `@M023` |
| `@X070` | **the rig holds TWO booms** — `boom` is what the player asked for (the wheel writes it), `cam.distance` is what the eye HAS after easing and occlusion.  ⚠ Collapse them and a wall the vehicle drove past permanently rewrites the zoom.  ⚠ Rest also SNAPS: an exponential approach is asymptotic, so stopping at a tolerance leaves the resting eye a function of how the frames fell — moros measured a 0.025-unit spread from exactly that | `RENDERER.md` § R2b, `@M024` |
| `@X071` | **ONE sight walker, in `passable.loft`, and it answers WHERE rather than whether** — `sight_first_block` returns the blocking cell's index, `tower_sees` is the boolean door onto it and `camera_boom_free` the distance door.  ⚠ It walks BOTH endpoints, which `tower_sees` used to skip: a looker's eye sits on top of its own hex, so the skip was never load-bearing — and the camera's far endpoint is the EYE, which is exactly the case that must be checkable | `RENDERER.md` § R2b, `ARCHITECTURE.md` |
| `@X072` | ⚠⚠ **the terrain mesh does NOT blend — flat tops and vertical sides, no corner-height mean.**  `@X044` (moros's mean of the hex and the neighbours sharing a corner) is what makes terrain slope instead of step, and in dryopea it is a **no-op at every hex in both directions**: `height_override` is non-null on exactly two of twelve palette kinds, so across ground every term of the mean is 0, and across a structure's edge `@X045`'s `faced_between` skips it anyway.  ⚠ **And it is honest rather than cheap**: the simulation asks `can_step`, a height DIFFERENCE, so a mesh that sloped where the rule steps would draw a ramp the vehicle cannot climb — flat tops make a height difference readable as the SIZE OF A QUAD, which is what the player is deciding about.  ⚠ The trigger to revisit is [`plan 02`](../plans/02-solver-validation-viewer/README.md)'s slope solver, and `plans/25` M2's halo gate is the tripwire | `plans/25` § What was measured first |
| `@X073` | **the corner↔direction relation is `hex_grid::hex_edge_corners`, delegated and never tabulated** — a side quad needs the two corners bounding the edge towards neighbour `dir`, the library already answers it, and a hand-written table would be a second lattice.  ⚠ The same discipline as `lat_neighbour` being the only stepper, and moros#10 is what a second lattice costs | `plans/25` § What was measured first, `lattice.loft` |
| `@X075` | ⚠⚠ **the drawn region is the painted set PLUS A ONE-HEX RING, and the mesher has its own tiling with no `ChunkField` in it.**  `painted.loft` ERASES a hex painted `sea`, so "mesh the painted set" leaves an erased region as a HOLE in the ground at exactly the height of the land around it — and no side quad covers it, because sea is 0 m and so is grass.  ⚠ The two rejected candidates each fail on a principle: the painted set's **bounding box** draws a quarter-million hexes of ocean between two distant bases, and the **tile's full extent** makes how far the ocean reaches a function of where the tile boundaries fell — which is `plans/22`'s *granularity must not follow the implementation* in a new place.  ⚠ **Its limit is asserted, not assumed**: an erased gap wider than two hexes still holes, and a test pins it so closing it is deliberate.  ⚠ The real answer is water's **DROP**, which the palette already carries per kind and nothing reads — a SIMULATION decision, so it belongs with [`plan 02`](../plans/02-solver-validation-viewer/README.md).  ⚠⚠ And **two reaches share the value 1 without sharing a constant**: `MESH_HALO_K` (how far an edit's consequences reach) and the drawn ring (how far past the paint anything is drawn) move independently the day a blend lands.  ⚠ No `ChunkField`: `collect_dirty_inputs` **skips a dirty chunk owning no cells** — which with a ring is a tile that still has sea to draw — and `mark_borders` steps chunk coords rectangularly, where `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is that only `lat_neighbour` steps a coordinate.  `mesh_chunks_touched` is ONE mechanism read two ways: as an edit it is the dirty rule, summed over the paint it is the tile list | `plans/25` § M2, `mesh_chunks.loft` |
| `@X074` | ⚠⚠ **colour is a UNIFORM and there is one mesh per palette kind** — moros's `chunk_mesh_mat(…, want_mat)` shape, and NOT a performance choice.  A flat-unlit frame drawn with a palette colour as a uniform **can only contain palette colours**, which is what keeps `@M002`'s exact classification alive; a per-vertex colour would interpolate across the triangle and every gradient pixel would land in `unknown` as a FAULT.  ⚠ So `RENDERER.md` § Open 3's *"flat unlit render mode for the gate"* is not a mode added later — it is the only way the mesher is written.  ⚠ `mesh3d::Vertex` carries no colour anyway, which is the shape agreeing rather than a gap | `plans/25` § M0, `RENDERER.md` § R4 |
| `@X076` | **the GL gate is a SEPARATE entry — `scripts/validate_gl.sh`, never rows in `scripts/validate.sh`.**  Plan 21 § R4's literal reading (*"validate.sh gains GL scenarios under xvfb"*) puts all **33** existing scripts behind an X server, and `RENDERER.md` § R0's probe went out of its way to prove the readback needs no display — *"or it could not run in CI either."*  ⚠ So a machine with no xvfb still runs the gate it can, and 654 headless measurements never wait on a GL context.  ⚠ Its consequence is a RULE: `.keys` has no GL verb (a `frame` command needing a context would drag all 33 scripts with it), so a fixture's expectations live in `src/gl_gate.loft` and a fixture with no case there is REFUSED by name rather than skipped | `plans/25` § M3, `gl_gate.loft` |
| `@X077` | ⚠⚠ **the GL gate's background is NOT a palette colour, and the gate's camera therefore looks at nothing but ground.**  A 3-D frame of a finite world is mostly background, so `other == 0` needs either a classified clear colour or a frame with no background in it.  ⚠ The first is REFUSED: an erased gap draws a HOLE (`@X075`), a hole shows the clear colour, and those pixels landing in `unknown` is the only thing that makes `@X075`'s named limit visible to a gate.  ⚠ So the clear colour is magenta — `palette_color`'s own "unknown kind" answer, outside the twelve buckets by construction as the palette grows — and `tests/gl/the-ground.keys` paints past the frustum, with `sea == 0` as the assertion that says so.  ⚠ A future fixture that cannot fill the frame cannot use `other == 0`; `an-island` is the worked example of what it asserts instead | `plans/25` § M3, `gl_gate.loft` |
| `@X078` | ⚠⚠ **a per-kind pixel count CANNOT see a mirrored world, so the GL gate also asks WHERE two uniquely-coloured hexes landed.**  Counts are permutation-invariant; `render_camera.loft` calls a mirrored world the failure that *"reads as a base that is its own reflection rather than as an error"* — so a gate built only of counts is blind to the defect that file is most afraid of (`@M027` measures it at 490.8 px past every green count).  The landmark is compared against **`camera_screen`**, the same function `@M022` measured the overview against the editor with, which chains drawn pixels → camera → software rasteriser and states `@X010` as something a frame can fail.  ⚠ A landmark must be **FLAT**: a column that stands draws its sides in the same colour, and those sides sit between the top face and the screen centre, so its centroid is not its centre — measured at **29 px** for a 5 m `wall_high` against **0.6 px** for a flat hex | `plans/25` § M3, `@M027`, `@X010` |
| `@X079` | ⚠⚠ **the fixed step's base unit is 1/3 of a MICROSECOND (3 000 000 a second), and microseconds were REFUSED on a measurement.**  A fixed step has to be an exact integer of some base unit or the construction collapses back into rounding — and dryopea's tick is 2/3 of a second, so `1e6 / 1.5` is **666 666.67** and no integer number of µs is it.  ⚠ `plans/26` § Open questions recommended **666 667 µs**, *"today's tick to within 1e-6 s"*, which *"moves nothing"*; measured, it moves **seventeen tests** (`@M031`).  ⚠ 1/3 µs is the COARSEST unit in which 2/3 of a second is whole — today's step is **2 000 000** units — so `TICK_SECONDS` derived from it is **bit-identical** to the `1.0 / 1.5` it replaced and there is ONE definition of the tick with no residue to document.  ⚠⚠ **The unit is the CONSUMER's choice, not the library's**: `clock_new` takes a step and the accumulator counts whatever the caller counts, because a game at 128 Hz cannot use 3 000 000 either (it is not divisible).  What `tick_clock.loft` promises is the IDENTITY, never a unit.  ⚠ The wall clock hands out µs and `clock_units_from_micros` is `* 3` — a multiplication, so the boundary `plans/26` § 1 found dryopea *"dividing the exactness away at"* is now exact | `plans/26` § L1, `tick_clock.loft`, `@M031`, `@X058` |
| `@X080` | ⚠⚠ **a `Bank` holds the CARRY and nothing else — the rate arrives per call and the WHOLE is a parameter.**  Plan 26 L2 replaced three hand-rolled *do-not-lose-a-fraction* sites (and the fourth that was missing, `@D003`) with one exact integer accumulator, and the two things it deliberately does NOT store are what make it usable.  ⚠ **The rate** is out because `@X061` says a rate is a property of a mover's CONDITION rather than of its class — *a damaged robot moves slower* — so `bank_gain(b, rate, elapsed, whole)` takes it every call.  ⚠⚠ **`whole` is out because loft defaults an omitted struct field SILENTLY** ([loft#914]) and dryopea builds `Enemy` from partial literals in a dozen places: a `Bank` carrying its own scale would default to 0 and make every one of them a mover that never moves, where a defaulted CARRY of 0 is a fresh bank and exactly right.  The price is one extra argument at three call sites inside `src/`, and no test ever writes it.  ⚠ **The rate is SCALED and the time is not**: time is already exact (`@X079`), a rate is an authored float, so `bank_rate` quantises to millionths — a tolerance on the RATE, never on the carry, which is the distinction the deleted epsilon blurred.  ⚠⚠ **The reciprocal form was refused on arithmetic**: storing *base units per whole unit* would make `Bank` into `clock_advance` with a variable step and need no scale at all, but `3 000 000 / 2.25` is 1 333 333.33 and `@M013` already sweeps 2.25 hex/s.  A rate scaled UP is exact for every authored number; a rate inverted is not — which is why `Bank` is not `TickClock` wearing a different name | `plans/26` § L2, `tick_bank.loft`, `@X061`, `@X079`, `@D003` |
| `@X081` | ⚠ **`vehicle_hexes_per_tick` is a CEILING that spends nothing, and it is a separate door from `vehicle_bank`.**  Once the player banks its ground, *how far may I point ahead* and *how far did I go* stop being one question: `play.loft::play_steer_reach` asks the first once per FRAME — sixty times a tick at 60 fps — so spelling it as a release would drain the player's carry just for holding a key down.  ⚠ The ceiling answers 2 cruising and 4 boosting at today's tick, exactly as the truncating version did, and answers **1** rather than 0 at a tick too short to guarantee a hex — which is the honest answer to *how far ahead*, and the one a release cannot give | `plans/26` § L2, `tick_bank.loft::bank_most`, `vehicle.loft` |
| `@X082` | ⚠⚠ **a `Timer` holds its `total` where a `Bank` may not hold its `whole` — and the rule that forbids one is the rule that permits the other.**  loft defaults an omitted struct field SILENTLY ([loft#914]), so the question a nested struct has to answer is *what does a defaulted instance MEAN at every site that builds a partial literal*.  For `Bank` the answer is fatal — a `whole` of 0 is a mover that never moves (`@X080`) — and for `Timer` it is exactly right: `total == 0` is UNARMED, which is what every `0.0` seconds field it replaces already meant (`recover: 0.0` is not recovering, `stand: 0.0` is free to walk, `lull: 0.0` is send now, `boost_left: 0.0` is not boosting).  ⚠ Holding `total` is also what lets ONE number answer BOTH directions — `timer_left` is `total − spent` — which is what deletes the direction problem rather than guarding it: there are no longer two accumulations to disagree.  ⚠⚠ **The family boundary was ATTACKED and held**: a one-shot built on `bank_gain` at `1/T` per second fires a SECOND time with nobody re-arming it, and its residue leaks into the next arming — a 5.0 s cooldown costs 8 ticks the first time and **7** the second.  Both failures are silent, which is why two small exact types beat one clever one | `plans/26` § L3, `tick_timer.loft`, `@X080`, `@M033` |
| `@X083` | ⚠⚠ **a POLICY is a DOOR beside the arithmetic, never a setting inside it — and the cap DROPS time rather than deferring or compressing it.**  Plan 26 L4 added the three things dryopea has no use for (a backlog cap, a rate scale, clock composition) because `plans/26` § FLEXIBLE's admission test is *could a consumer be tempted to hand-roll this?* and moros had already built two of them.  ⚠ **Each is a separate function over the ONE accumulator**, so `clock_advance` is untouched and `play_advance` stays uncapped — five entry points over five copies of the arithmetic would be § 2's seven-sites problem wearing a library's clothes.  ⚠⚠ **`clock_advance_capped` throws the excess AWAY**: the steps past the cap are not owed, so the frame after a stall answers 1 rather than a burst.  The version a driver writes by mistake clamps the answer and leaves the backlog banked — a DEFERRAL, which pays the stall off over the following frames and runs the simulation behind the wall for ever; both versions answer *fewer ticks* on the stalled frame, so the plan's stated control cannot tell them apart and the deferring driver is reproduced beside the shipped one as the real one (`@M034`).  ⚠ And nothing here can COMPRESS — the step is fixed and the door answers a COUNT — which is moros's own *a compressed catch-up is the variable-step bug wearing a bound's clothes*.  ⚠ A cap below 1 is clamped to 1 for `clock_new`'s reason: a simulation that can never advance is a mistake, not a mode; **pause is a rate of 0**.  ⚠ `clock_drive` is the honest exception — `n * step` at the call site is already exact because both sides are integers, so the door earns its place by NAMING the case rather than by fixing it, which is the opposite of `play_ticks` (wrong for 602 of the first 1000 `n`) and is asserted as such | `plans/26` § L4, `tick_clock.loft`, `@M034`, `19-P1` |
| `@X084` | ⚠⚠ **a clock's RATE is a RATIONAL, applied at ONE private site, to every DURATION door and to NO count door.**  `num / den` with the wall remainder carried in `[0, den)` — pause is `0 / 1`, half-speed `1 / 2` — so a rate that does not divide loses nothing for ever, where the hand-rolled `elapsed * 0.3333…` a consumer reaches for drifts against a rate it could not state in the first place (measured: a wall minute at a third of speed is **30** ticks exact and **29** truncated, `@M034`).  ⚠ It is `tick_bank.loft` § THE RATE IS SCALED AND THE TIME IS NOT one family over, with the opposite conclusion about exactness: a mover's rate is authored in `numbers.json` as a float and must be quantised (`@X080`), a clock's rate is authored by a KEYPRESS and can be stated exactly.  ⚠⚠ **The count door is exempt and that is the load-bearing half** — a script's `tick 30`, a turn being committed and a rollback re-simulation have no wall clock to slow down, and a paused game unable to run its own tests would be a mode flag wearing a rate's clothes.  ⚠ **A second accumulator, and it is a different QUESTION rather than a second copy of one**: `banked` carries sim time toward the next step, `rate_carry` carries wall time toward the next sim unit; folding them by scaling `banked` would make `clock_banked`'s UNIT change with the rate, and it is a save/restore value.  ⚠⚠ **A defaulted rate of `0 / 0` reads as UNSCALED, which is this plan's THIRD answer to one loft rule** ([loft#914], a silently defaulted field): a `Bank` may not hold its `whole` because a defaulted 0 is a mover that never moves (`@X080`), a `Timer` MAY hold its `total` because a defaulted 0 is unarmed (`@X082`), and here the defaulted value IS *the behaviour you already had* — `plans/26` § The two rules under all of it, stated as data rather than as prose.  ⚠ A negative `num` is clamped to 0 rather than rewinding: time going backwards is dropped everywhere else in the clock, and a reversing rate would need every consumer to own an inverse tick | `plans/26` § L4, `tick_clock.loft`, `@X080`, `@X082`, `@M034` |
| `@X085` | ⚠⚠ **the ALPHA is the clock's and the POLICY is the renderer's — and an eased follow camera never owned the mover in the first place.**  Plan 26 L5 shipped `clock_alpha` (the bank as a fraction of the step, half-open by construction) and `play_alpha`, and **no interpolation policy at all**.  ⚠⚠ **The measured reason it is not the clock's call**: three policies, three prices on three different axes — `lerp(prev, cur, alpha)` is smooth and **one whole step behind for ever** (2.598 m here), `cur + alpha·(cur − prev)` is **exact to 9.5e-16 m** while the velocity holds and jumps a whole step's travel whenever it changes (2.598 m starting, 2.533 m stopping), and a first-order ease needs no previous state, lags between them and is the only one whose picture depends on the frames it was drawn on.  **Which is right is a function of how long the STEP is**, so a clock that picked one would be wrong for every consumer whose step is a different length (`@M035`).  ⚠⚠ **And the second half is about WHERE it is applied**: the shipped ease does not remove a lattice mover's jump, it moves it off the world and onto the mover, so alpha on the mover alone leaves a seventh of it behind — *the mover and the camera's target are one decision, not two*.  ⚠ Nothing is built today because nothing DRAWS an entity today; a previous position per drawn thing would be a field nobody reads, and [`plans/20`](../plans/20-entity-art/README.md) inherits both halves | `plans/26` § L5, `tick_clock.loft`, `play.loft`, `@M035`, `@M023` |
| `@X086` | ⚠⚠ **the timing arithmetic is a PACKAGE in `loft-libs-game`, and the admission test that put it there is *every game needs this* rather than *two games wrote it*.**  Plan 26 L6 moved `tick_clock.loft`, `tick_bank.loft` and `tick_timer.loft` out of `src/` unchanged, plus the camera's ease out of `render_camera.loft`, into `fixstep 0.1.0` — zero dependencies, 13 tests, one per door.  ⚠ **A move, not a rewrite**, and that is the gate: dryopea's **1322 tests and 654 measurements are green and UNMOVED** across it.  ⚠⚠ **`loft-libs-game` rather than a standalone repo** (project owner, 2026-08-17: *"something like this is needed for every game that is built"*) — the surveyed evidence found only ONE other duplicated candidate (`approach`, which ships inside the package), so a themed repo looked like over-building on duplication alone; the owner's test is the stronger one and that chunk already exists for *runtime game services … consumed by every game*.  ⚠ The chunk's README had advertised the slot since bootstrap — its `time/` row reads *"frame counter, dt, scheduling"* while `time` shipped as date arithmetic — so this fills a package that was planned and never landed.  ⚠ **`moros_map` is NOT a second tenant**: it is a world model and belongs beside `hex_world` in `loft-libs-world`.  ⚠ **`tick` was refused twice over** — `ticks()` is a loft builtin, AND moros already has a `tick` module that is a Walker rather than a clock.  ⚠⚠ **The example half is gated and it caught itself**: a tag in a section header is separated from its `fn` by a blank line, which breaks the binding, so all thirteen citations were dangling while looking correct | `plans/26` § L6, `loft-libs-game/fixstep`, `@X079`, `@X080`, `@X082` |

### play — progression ([`PROGRESSION.md`](PROGRESSION.md))

| code | decision | owner |

| `@X016` | ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-26 by `@X111`** — ~~the progression is SKILL, not stats~~.  It was a session's derivation, not the owner's design: *"progression should not be about player skill … there can be multiple dimensions"*.  ⚠ What survives is that **what the player KNOWS and DECIDES is the dominant axis** (`@X117`) — a different claim, about information rather than dexterity | `PROGRESSION.md` § P0, § P1 |
| `@X017` | ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-26 by `@X116`** — ~~the player's vehicle must not get faster~~.  The same over-correction: what constrains a vehicle upgrade is the FRICTION fence, not a ban.  ⚠ A vehicle that carries more, climbs better or sees further passes; a vehicle that FIGHTS still does not (`DESIGN.md` § 8) | `PROGRESSION.md` § P3, § P6 |
| `@X018` | the **landscape is the school** and the fiction already made it safe — both non-human tiers open as maintenance.  ⚠ Still true, and after `@X117` it is where the DOMINANT axis is learned rather than where a dexterity is drilled | `PROGRESSION.md` § P1, § G2 |
| `@X019` | the **base layout is the exam** — ⚠ **and since 2026-08-26 it is examined on three axes**: what the player KNOWS (a sealed wall doubles the clock, a gate buys nothing), what they can FLY, and what their crew can SERVICE.  The measured racing line is intact; it is no longer the only reading | `PROGRESSION.md` § P7, `@M004` |

### run — exploration and the session clock ([`EXPLORATION.md`](EXPLORATION.md))

| code | decision | owner |
|---|---|---|
| `@X020` | **exploration IS scouting** — already *the* progression activity; this design assembles rather than adds a pillar | `EXPLORATION.md` banner |
| `@X021` | **the run already opens with a sortie** (`wave_provoke_step`), so "explore earlier" is CONTENT on a trip the player already takes | `EXPLORATION.md` § X2 |
| `@X022` | ⚠⚠ **the game WAITS** — an unlimited recon window the player ends deliberately by poking a marker | `EXPLORATION.md` § X2b |
| `@X023` | **the four robot classes gate the intel layer** — until they exist every wave is the same wave and a sortie predicts nothing.  ⚠ One row each in `numbers.json` + one branch in the damage-to-wall lookup.  ⚠ **HALF DONE** — the wall-damage axis shipped as `23-K0` and was exactly that cheap; the SPEED axis is `23-K2` and is not a row (see `@X053`) | `EXPLORATION.md` § X2b, `plans/23` |
| `@X053` | ⚠ **the harvester's role is what it CARRIES** — its body is `RUBBLE_CARGO` and cargo pays **3×**, so the one hole `DESIGN.md` § Small robots left open is filled by `body_source` → `loot_rate`, two lookups that already composed.  ⚠ Chosen against the genre test: at 90 points a body, letting one through and collecting it later is a real alternative to killing it at the wall | owner, 2026-08-15; `plans/23` § Open questions 1 |
| `@X054` | ⚠ **`robot` is not retired and is not one of the four** — the small robots APPEND as kinds 3-6 and the baseline keeps 1.0 HP/s, which is what let four classes land without moving one of 1 094 tests or 520 measurements | `plans/23` § K0 |
| `@X055` | ⚠⚠ **a wave's SIZE is SUMMED from its parts, never stored** — a wave is a flat `vector<WavePart>` keyed by wave index, so *the parts sum to the count* is the definition rather than an invariant.  ⚠ This **DELETES** `plans/23`'s own named negative control (*"a mix whose parts do not sum to the wave's count is refused at parse"*): the check only exists if the count is a second fact beside the parts, which is the shape `carry.loft` refuses.  ⚠ `wave_schedule_of(vector<integer>)` keeps its meaning — N waves of REGULARS — which is what let composition land with **569 measurements unchanged** | `plans/23` § K1, `waves.loft` § A wave is PARTS |
| `@X056` | **`schedule` arms and `compose` fills** — two `.keys` verbs rather than one grammar, because `schedule 12 12` already means *two waves of twelve* and a form that also took class names could not tell a wave boundary from a group boundary.  ⚠ `compose` REPLACES a wave, so re-running a fixture reaches the same state; and a `schedule` line AFTER a `compose` wipes it, which is why `emit.loft`'s order is a requirement | `plans/23` § K1 |
| `@X057` | ⚠ **`WaveFile` is deliberately NOT given composition** — nothing in `src/` loads it, and the shape it would need is `vector<Struct>` through a `text as`, which is the cast that hangs and miscompiles to zero entries on native.  Composition is authored in the `.keys` vocabulary, where dryopea authors everything else about a run | `plans/23` § K1, `plans/23` § Open questions 3 |
| `@X058` | ⚠⚠ **the tick's duration is no longer DERIVED, it is CHOSEN** — an enemy banks `speed × tick_seconds` and steps when a whole hex is due, so `TICK_SECONDS = 1/ENEMY_SPEED_HEX_PER_SECOND` is now what HOLDS the timestep at one regular's hex rather than what forces it.  ⚠ The expression stays because changing it moves all 569 measurements for a reason that is not a defect | `plans/23` § K2a, `spawn.loft` § What a tick is worth |
| `@X059` | ⚠ **a hex the ground refuses is SPENT, not re-banked** — the OPPOSITE of `helper_bank`, and deliberate: a helper parked at a wall keeps accumulating so its average stays a rate, but an enemy stopped by a wall is BESIEGING, and storing the hexes would let a robot that chewed a breach for ten ticks cross the base the moment it opens | `plans/23` § K2a |
| `@X060` | ⚠ **`enemy_bank` takes its SPEED as an argument**, where `helper_bank` reads a constant — because 1.5 hex/s is one of the few speeds at which the rounding guard cannot fire, so a bank that read the constant could only ever be tested at the value that hides its own guard | `plans/23` § K2a, `@M013` |
| `@X061` | ⚠ **the class lookup lives at the CALL SITE, not in the bank** — `enemy_tick` asks `enemy_speed(e.kind)` and hands the number over, so `enemy_bank` still knows only a rate.  The reason has outlived `@X060`'s: `DESIGN.md` § Speed must NOT be tied to the tick wants speed to vary *within a life* (*"a damaged robot moves slower"*), which makes the number a property of an enemy's CONDITION rather than of its class — so the one line that decides pace has to be somewhere a condition can also be read | `plans/23` § K2b, `spawn.loft` § How fast each class arrives |
| `@X062` | ⚠ **harvester and builder get NO speed row**, and that is `numbers.json`'s DELTAS shape rather than an omission: a role that lists no field takes the regular's.  Giving all four a row would invent two tunables to say *unchanged* | `plans/23` § K2b, `NUMBERS.md` |
| `@X063` | ⚠⚠ **2.25 and 3.0 hex/s were refused for a ROUNDING reason, not a design one** — both are *"quite a bit faster"* and both make `speed × tick_seconds` exact, hiding `ENEMY_PROGRESS_EPSILON` exactly as 1.5 does (`@M013`).  Picking one would have left the whole roster unable to see its own rounding.  ⚠ The general rule: **when a constant's guard is invisible at the shipped value, the next value shipped should be one that can see it** | `plans/23` § K2b, `@M013`, `@M017` |
| `@X064` | ⚠⚠ **the equal-distance SIDESTEP is refused inside plan 23 and priced instead** — `@M018` measures a mixed wave collapsing to its fastest class because only three robots can ever reach the wall, and the fix is a besieger spreading ALONG the face.  ⚠ It is a second steering rule, so it is a PLAN; and a measurement phase that changed the thing it was measuring would have measured nothing.  ⚠ The general rule: **when a measurement finds the cause of what it measured, its job is to price the fix, not to apply it** | `plans/23` § K3, `@M018`, `ENEMY_MOVEMENT.md` § The siege front is three hexes wide |
| `@X024` | **a find is a BUILD ACCELERANT**, and what decays is *the opportunity to use it* — so it must be found early | `EXPLORATION.md` § X2c, `@M006` |
| `@X025` | **the PERMIT is the run clock**, and the cordon's teeth are at the exit.  ⚠ Expiry costs the **CARGO**, never the run — § 14 has no fail screen | `EXPLORATION.md` § X2d |
| `@X026` | a find is **one marker row + one cargo row**, and nothing else.  ⚠ A find that needs a subsystem has become an economy | `EXPLORATION.md` § X4 |
| `@X027` | **every find opens a fight**, and the fight is a wave that already exists — no new mover | `EXPLORATION.md` § X5 |
| `@X028` | **the first find is a stranded helper** — it needs no code at all, and it is a build accelerant whose value compounds | `EXPLORATION.md` § X7 |
| `@X029` | **what persists is INTEL** — a per-node `found` flag, not revealed pixels.  ⚠ No fog-of-war layer | `EXPLORATION.md` § X8 |

### run — the setting and the seed hand-over ([`SETTING.md`](SETTING.md), [`MATERIALS.md`](MATERIALS.md))

⚠ Folded 2026-08-26 from [`../archive/seed-notes.md`](../archive/seed-notes.md)
and [`../archive/gameplay.data`](../archive/gameplay.data); routed block by
block in [`DESIGN_HISTORY.md`](DESIGN_HISTORY.md) §§ 4-5.

| code | decision | owner |
|---|---|---|
| `@X099` | ⚠⚠ **the jammer forbids remote operation, and that is the game's charter** — *"very little communication with the surface possible during a mission, so there has to be personnel below to service the different systems"*.  The equipment that makes the robots beatable is what puts humans on the ground, so every servicing loop the project has measured exists because of one sentence in the recruitment call.  ⚠ It is what refuses a power grid, automated transport, and any comms upgrade that works under the scrambler | `SETTING.md` § The recruitment |
| `@X100` | **the pollen is the environmental cause of the repair economy** — aggressive to humans AND machines, so repair is a condition rather than an event.  ⚠ It must stay a CONSTANT and never become a weather event with a timer, or it is a lean-back mechanic | `SETTING.md` § The pollen |
| `@X101` | ⚠ **rock blocks radio**, which is why the AI is a surface power, why the natives were never found, and what the player's 25-hex bubble is imitating.  ⚠ It is also why a cave is a hiding place rather than a shortcut — a place the enemy cannot reach is a place the game does not happen | `SETTING.md` § Rock blocks radio |
| `@X102` | ⚠⚠ **the knowledge tree is the one accumulating progression that passes the genre test** — ~55 facts in per-faction arcs.  ⚠ The boundary: knowledge changes what the player can **ATTEMPT** and what the map will SHOW them, never how well they do it.  *Cave entrances* makes a scan exist; it does not make scanning better | `SETTING.md` § The knowledge tree, `PROGRESSION.md` § P4 |
| `@X103` | ⚠⚠ **REVERSED 2026-08-26 by `@X111`** — ~~the 2023 RPG layer is refused~~.  **The owner's ruling: *"the RPG part here will win"*.**  ⚠ Recorded rather than deleted because the refusal stood for one day and the mistake is instructive: it read *a stat* as *a route to winning by personal power*, which `@X118` separates | `PROGRESSION.md` § P0 |
| `@X104` | ⚠⚠ **a POWER GRID is refused for the player's base** — a wire replaces a drive, and `plans/17` § T3 measured that the drive IS the game (two shuttling helpers clear 205 robots; the same two parked lose the base).  ⚠ Generators are fine: a source with a position is the existing mechanic with new flavour.  ⚠ The **enemy** may have the full grid — a power line between installations is one more edge to cut | `MATERIALS.md` § Power |
| `@X105` | ⚠ **automated transport is refused for the player and welcomed for the enemy** — *"maximize automatic transport of materials"* removes the trip.  `ROBOT_ECONOMY.md` § 3 already makes a rail a route to cut, which is the version that adds a decision instead of deleting one | `MATERIALS.md` § What this design does NOT do |
| `@X106` | ⚠ **a material tree passes the genre test only because materials have WEIGHT and are SOMEWHERE ELSE** — getting one is a trip.  A tree that resolves into a menu is the failure mode.  ⚠ Ship **three** materials, not the 2023 catalogue's 34; the rest arrive when a mechanic asks for one by name | `MATERIALS.md` § The governing rule, § Open questions 3 |
| `@X107` | ⚠ **the AI core is the one part that cannot be manufactured, only taken** — and a recycler must never break one down, or the highest-value thing in the game becomes a quantity | `MATERIALS.md` § Parts |
| `@X108` | ⚠⚠ **a trap that does not auto-reset passes the design test outright** — placed in advance, fires once, then somebody drives out mid-wave to re-arm it.  Mechanically a black tower restored by a standing vehicle (`plans/17` T1) with the cost moved to the front | `MATERIALS.md` § Towers, `plans/ROADMAP.md` Tier B |
| `@X109` | ⚠ **a FLYER is blocked and a wall-climbing SPIDER costs one row** — `ENEMY_MOVEMENT.md`'s *ONE AI, per-class DATA*: a flyer needs its own mover and deletes the wall, while `can_climb` already takes the limit as a parameter | `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` § 4c |
| `@X110` | ⚠ **the six damage TYPES stay six** — the 2023 sheet's twelve are not an upgrade to `DESIGN.md` § Damage TYPE's triangle.  ⚠ Two of them contribute: `falloff` is *range is a PROFILE, not a number* named, and `poison` is *"ignored by machines"*, i.e. a purely anti-insect axis | `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` § 5d |
| `@X111` | ⚠⚠ **the crew are CHARACTERS — the 2023 RPG layer is ADOPTED** (owner, 2026-08-26): twelve skills, six statistics each bonusing four of them, endurance pools that rest restores, backgrounds and classes.  ⚠ `DESIGN.md` § 9 has carried a per-helper id since plan 14 *"so future skills hang off existing characters"* — the data model was already waiting | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2 |
| `@X112` | ⚠⚠ **a skill SCALES A NUMBER THAT ALREADY EXISTS** — `build` scales helper-seconds (the bottleneck `DESIGN.md` § 13 already names), `repair` the 20 s standing clock, `scout` the detection radius, `scrounge` `loot_rate`.  ⚠ So no skill introduces a mechanism, and the layer lands one skill at a time against numbers that are already measured.  ⚠⚠ **BUILT for the first three, 2026-08-27 (BACKLOG C1, `@X278`) — and one of the four claims here was FALSE**: *the detection radius* did not exist, in `src/` or in `numbers.json`, which is the case this row itself says to push back on.  `@X277` is the owner's ruling on what it actually is, and `scout` cost a mechanism rather than a multiply | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2 |
| `@X113` | **endurance POOLS, spent by work and restored by rest** — the crew-side version of the tower's charge.  ⚠ It is what makes `MATERIALS.md`'s *living quarters* a real building: a sortie long enough to need rest must build for it.  ⚠ And *"personnel can join competitors when not treated well"* makes neglect a run-level consequence with a face on it | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2d |
| `@X114` | **the 28 upgrades are design material** — reversed with the rest.  ⚠ Two still need a ruling and it is `@X118`'s rather than a blanket one: the **laser communicator** survives only as a LINE-OF-SIGHT relay (a general radio deletes `@X099`), and the **auto pilot** keeps its authored drawback because it automates the axis the player enjoys | `PROGRESSION.md` § P3 |
| `@X115` | ⚠ **the perspective rule** — the world is seen through a person, and switching to another happens only at the main communication terminal.  ⚠ With `@X111` adopted this stops being a curiosity: without it a skilled crew is a spreadsheet the player watches, and with it the switch costs a drive back to the core mid-wave.  ⚠ Designed, not decided — it changes the control model (`DESIGN.md` § 8, § 9) | `PROGRESSION.md` § P8 |
| `@X116` | ⚠⚠ **the model is BLUE PRINCE** (owner, 2026-08-26): information and the player's own build choices DOMINATE progression, *"but there is still a lot of room for game progression that gradually makes traversing/building easier"*.  ⚠ Two progressions that never compete because they answer different questions — and the practical consequence is that **the friction-reducers can be generous** | `PROGRESSION.md` § P0 |
| `@X117` | ⚠⚠ **the dominant axis is what the player KNOWS and DECIDES**, not what they can execute — laying out a base is dryopea's DRAFT.  ⚠ *A sealed wall doubles the clock and a gate buys nothing* (`@M004`) is the purest example: no upgrade grants it, no stat substitutes for it, and a player who has it plays differently on day one of a new run.  ⚠ Execution (boost lines, taking a line under pressure) is real and is the MINOR kind | `PROGRESSION.md` § P1a, § P1b |
| `@X118` | ⚠⚠ **the fence is FRICTION vs ANSWERS** — *does this reduce the cost of acting on what the player knows, or does it supply what they do not know?*  A shortcut saves you steps; it never tells you the combination.  ⚠ **Build speed, repair speed and detection radius all pass**; a layout the game recommends, a readout that NAMES the incoming composition, or salvage without the trip all fail.  ⚠ The failing column is not *too strong* — it is **doing the player's thinking** | `PROGRESSION.md` § P6a |
| `@X119` | ⚠ **and the player stays SMALL** (owner, 2026-08-14) — no route to *become strong enough and win*.  ⚠ Much narrower than the refusal it replaces: it forbids a weapon skill that lets one person hold a perimeter alone, and hacking that converts a boss into a bodyguard.  **It does not forbid a stat** | `PROGRESSION.md` § P6b, `DESIGN.md` § And the DEEP layers |
| `@X120` | ⚠⚠ **A RETURNING PLAYER MAY SKIP THE RAMP, AND NOTHING MAY STOP THEM** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"it is still possible to make huge progress in the first mission when a player decides to restart the game after a long campaign"*.  ⚠ So **the first base may not be balanced for a naive player**, and content may not be gated on progress the player has already earned in their head.  ⚠⚠ Its corollary is a rule for every unlock: **prefer FOUND over AWARDED** — `DESIGN.md` § Scouting already finds new tower types on the map, and a veteran knows where to look, where *"clear three missions"* is impermeable to knowledge | `PROGRESSION.md` § P1c |
| `@X121` | **the second progression's job is SMOOTHING** — *"after doing many scenarios the game helps the player to get to content/actions easier later in the game"*.  ⚠ It is not difficulty compensation and not a power curve: it is what stops a long campaign spending its minutes on problems the player solved twenty sorties ago | `PROGRESSION.md` § P0c |
| `@X122` | ⚠⚠ **the RULES are learnable, the INSTANCE is not** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"enough random elements in each scenario that exploration stays a big part of it … you roughly know what to expect but cannot predict everything"*.  ⚠ Without it `@X117` eats itself: a dominant knowledge progression in a FIXED world is solved after enough runs.  ⚠⚠ **Vary the instance, never the rules** — where the ridge is, which classes are in the wave, which tree withered; never what a sealed wall is worth or how fast a miner chews (which is also why `numbers.json` is global and not per-map).  ⚠ And a veteran must be able to form good expectations and be surprised at the MARGIN — too much randomness destroys the dominant axis from the other side | `PROGRESSION.md` § P1d |
| `@X123` | ⚠ **scouting converts rules-knowledge into instance-knowledge, every run** — which is why exploration never goes stale and why the veteran scouts **faster and better rather than less**.  ⚠ It is also what makes `scout` / detection radius a legitimate friction-reducer under `@X118`: they make the conversion cheaper and convert nothing on their own | `PROGRESSION.md` § P1d, `EXPLORATION.md` |
| `@X124` | ⚠⚠ **the two layers are a LOOP — practice raises the STATISTIC, and the statistic permeates** (owner, 2026-08-26): a skill rises fast with use, its two statistics rise slowly, and those lift **six other skills** each.  ⚠ Measured off the 2023 data rather than retyped: 12 skills, 6 statistics, every skill bonused by exactly 2 and every statistic bonusing exactly 4, **diameter 2** — so no crew member ever dead-ends and the six-helper cap does not force redundancy.  ⚠⚠ It passes `@X118` automatically, because use-based advancement can only reward work the player already decided to do; and it is `@X121`'s smoothing **with no dial on it** | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2a |
| `@X125` | ⚠⚠ **TEMPLATES, never rerolls** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"I do not like the system where players have to do many rerolls to get the optimal NPC … we give them templates instead that can be optimized by actually letting the NPC perform related tasks"*.  ⚠ A reroll loop is `@X118`'s failure mode wearing a dice cup: the player pays time for the game to hand them a stat block.  ⚠⚠ **And do not randomise which templates are OFFERED** — that re-invents it one level up, and `@X122` does not license it, because a hiring pool is not a place you go and LOOK at | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2b |
| `@X126` | ⚠ **practice banks at the SCRAMBLE** — gains accrue during a sortie and count for a helper who boarded before force-launch, over the cargo manifest `DESIGN.md` § 14 already keeps.  ⚠⚠ So leaving somebody behind now costs their **experience** as well as their body, which sharpens the scramble decision rather than softening it — and it needs no new mechanism | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2a |
| `@X127` | ⚠ **advancement must not reward a NO-OP** — a use-based system invites grinding repairs on an undamaged tower.  dryopea's existing mechanics already enforce most of it (a **firing tower refuses repair**, salvage decays, the permit clocks the run), and the question to ask of every new skill is whether its practice can be repeated at zero cost | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2a |
| `@X128` | ⚠ **`combat` and `scout` are statistical TWINS** — both observe+stamina, the only collapsed pair in the lattice, which is why both permeate to 5 where every other skill reaches 6.  ⚠⚠ **dryopea should drop `combat`**: `DESIGN.md` § 8's noncombatant player leaves it almost nothing to scale, while `scout` scales the detection radius.  Dropping it orphans no statistic and changes no other skill's permeation.  ⚠ And a NEW skill should take one of the **four free pairs** (agility+stamina, charisma+observe, observe+plan, stamina+tinker) rather than duplicate an occupied one — duplication is how the twins happened | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2a |
| `@X129` | ⚠⚠ **the crew REMARK on the world, coloured by their past job** (owner, 2026-08-26) — an ex-miner, an ex-security officer, an ex-handyman and an ex-scout land with different skills and say different things.  ⚠⚠ **A remark POINTS, it never CONCLUDES**: *"soft ground on that ridge"*, never *"build here"*, and never `@X118`'s failing row (a layout the game recommends, a readout that NAMES the incoming wave).  ⚠⚠ **The crew are PARTIAL SENSORS** — each right about their own domain and blind to the rest, so following one exclusively is how a base goes wrong and the synthesis stays with the player (`@X117`).  ⚠ It PRESERVES the trip rather than removing it: a remark is a reason to go and look.  ⚠ And it is the skill sheet made diegetic — the player reads the roster by noticing who speaks up about what, with no character screen | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c, `DESIGN.md` § HUD, § 9 |
| `@X130` | ⚠⚠ **crew remarks are BLOCKED on text, and they are the first designed feature that is** — `@X097` says `draw_text` rasterises through a `#native` call unavailable under `loft test` and needs a font file this repo lacks, which is why the wallet is seven-segment rectangles.  ⚠ Everything else in the design routed around it (a colour, a shape, a pulse); a spoken line cannot.  **So the prerequisite is a font in the repo plus `draw_text` reachable under `loft test`**, or the remark is a thing no test and no `snap` can see.  ⚠ The buildable interim is a speaker and a subject drawn without words — the crew vehicle indicating, the subject outlined — strictly weaker, and gateable by pixels.  ⚠⚠ **UNBLOCKED 2026-08-27 by BACKLOG B1** (`@X268`, `@M047`): `assets/DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf` ships and `src/font.loft` is the seam, and half the blocker turned out to be **stale** — `draw_text` already drew headless.  ⚠ The interim is therefore no longer the plan.  ⚠⚠ What a remark still has to settle is **compositing**, not capability: anti-aliasing puts 58% of a glyph outside every palette colour, so a line drawn into the world frame breaks `@X077` and `@X092` | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c, `@X268` |
| `@X131` | ⚠⚠ **a relationship is a LEDGER of what happened, never an affinity bar** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"a shared history gets them together"*.  ⚠ It is the SAME MECHANISM as `@X124`: the crew get better at what they DID, and closer through what they WENT THROUGH — one system, two readouts.  ⚠⚠ **The player never raises it**; they run sorties and the crew remember, so there is nothing to farm (`@X125`) and no bar to draw (`DESIGN.md` § HUD).  ⚠ Their only influence is indirect and positional: **who they send together, and who they come back for** | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2e |
| `@X132` | ⚠⚠ **the history already EXISTS** — who retrieved whom (`DESIGN.md` § 9, shipped in `plans/14`), who was left behind at force-launch (§ 14, *"the cost of haste"*), who boarded (the cargo manifest `@X126` already banks on), which bases fell with you both in them.  ⚠ So the feature adds a record and not a simulation.  ⚠⚠ **And § 9's STRANDED HELPER becomes the campaign's best story beat with nothing written**: you left them because the wave was on the core, and going back is a decision about a person you know — the signature mechanic generating its own drama | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2e, `DESIGN.md` § 9 |
| `@X133` | ⚠ **a relationship MAY have a mechanical effect, through the lattice that already exists** — `social` is a skill and `charisma` a statistic, so a bond is the § P2a loop with shared work as the practice.  ⚠⚠ The passing form is narrow and it is all COVERAGE: two people who have worked together **hand a job over without re-walking it** (`plans/17` § T1 already banks repair ON THE TOWER for exactly that reason).  ⚠ What fails `@X118`: a bonded pair that unlocks an ability, a loyalty THRESHOLD granting a bonus, or a relationship GATE on any content | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2e |
| `@X134` | ⚠ **keep COUNTS and LANDMARKS, never a log** — an event log per crew pair grows for ever, which is the shape `@X095` already refused for a different subject.  ⚠ A few counts (sorties together, retrievals, abandonments) plus two or three landmark events kept **by name**, so a fifty-sortie campaign costs a fixed handful of fields per pair and a remark still has something concrete to point at.  ⚠⚠ Prerequisites are real: **per-campaign persistence** (ROADMAP Tier D, not shipped) and **text** (`@X130`), which a bond needs more than a remark does | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2e |
| `@X135` | ⚠⚠ **remarks fire in OFF-TIME, gated on the player's own state** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"the gentle nudge to a player that feels lost"*.  ⚠ A player getting on with it hears nothing; a player who has stalled hears one line.  ⚠⚠ **It satisfies `@X120` for free**: a veteran who never stalls never hears a hint, so the tutorial is skipped without a skip button, a difficulty setting or a toggle.  ⚠ The off-time is a phase that already exists — `@X022`'s unbounded recon window, which the player ends deliberately by poking a marker | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c |
| `@X136` | ⚠⚠ **SILENCE must be a real outcome, and it is the DEFENCE rather than modesty** — *"they do not hold all the answers"*.  If the crew always produce a useful line when the player stalls, **stalling becomes the way to get answers** and a player will stall deliberately: `@X118` violated through a back door, with waiting as the currency.  ⚠⚠ **And the trigger is the absence of PROGRESS, never idleness** — in dryopea *standing still is work*: a parked player is repairing (`plans/17` § T1, repair is a POSITION), so an idle detector would interrupt the most important job in the game.  ⚠ Bias the window LONG: firing early talks over a thinking player, firing late costs nothing because they solved it themselves.  ⚠ It may read the CAMERA where `@X033` forbids the simulation to — a nudge is presentation — provided nothing the simulation reads back is fed from it | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c |
| `@X137` | ⚠⚠ **THERE IS NO TUTORIAL** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"simple/visible enough controls that a player can just find by playing around"*.  No tutorial mission, no first-run overlay, no tooltip layer.  ⚠⚠ It is affordable because of `DESIGN.md` § Position triggers: **most of the game has no key to learn at all** — driving over loot picks it up, driving to a tower repairs it, standing on a spawn marker starts the run.  ⚠ Play mode is **five keys** (WASD, E, Q, Shift) plus the mouse | `DESIGN.md` § There is NO TUTORIAL |
| `@X138` | ⚠⚠ **the recon window IS the tutorial sandbox, and nobody built it for that** — `@X022`'s unbounded wait means a new player can wander, press every key and drive into everything with **nothing punishing them**, because `SETTING.md` § Nobody is attacking anybody makes the world safe until the player interferes and `wave_provoke_step` starts the run only when they say so.  ⚠ So three of a tutorial's four jobs were already answered (controls by position triggers, feedback by the diegetic HUD, safety by the wait) — and the fourth, **what to do first**, is what `@X129`'s remarks are for, which promotes them from a convenience to **the onboarding** | `DESIGN.md` § There is NO TUTORIAL, `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c |
| `@X139` | ⚠⚠ **the key table is a DESIGN BUDGET, and a gate already enforces it** — every key is a thing the player must discover unaided, so a new binding needs an argument rather than a row.  `tests/09_i1_bindings.loft` pins the ROW COUNT of `bindings.loft`'s one table, written as a regression check and now carrying the design meaning too.  ⚠⚠ **And it adds a TEST for every future mechanic**: *can a player find this by playing around?*  A mechanic that needs explaining needs **redesigning**, because there is nowhere left to explain it | `DESIGN.md` § There is NO TUTORIAL |
| `@X140` | ⚠ **two named discoverability hazards** — **Q** (wall-paint toggle) is the sharpest: § 11 already calls it *"acknowledged exception to the spatial principle"*, and **building is the biggest missing mechanic**, so a player who never presses Q never plays the actual game.  ⚠ **Shift** (boost) is second — `plans/13` § V4 measured it as the only way out of a sealed base — but the hazard is **ordered**: sealing yourself in requires a wall, which requires Q.  ⚠ Q's mitigation today is the red vehicle tint, obvious *once found*; finding it is what a landing remark is for | `DESIGN.md` § There is NO TUTORIAL |
| `@X141` | ⚠⚠ **a simple input scheme is NECESSARY and not SUFFICIENT** (owner, 2026-08-26) — *"not guarantee that the player will be engaged and have a clear view about what to do.  So that is where the helpers come in"*.  ⚠ Three distinct failures, and controls only reach the first: *I don't know how* (position triggers), *I don't know what is worth doing* (the crew, as information), *I have stopped caring* (the crew, as **engagement** — a different problem a control scheme can never reach).  ⚠⚠ **A silent world is where players drift**: a crew member remarking on where you stand is the world NOTICING you.  ⚠ And across a campaign it is `@X131`'s shared history that makes the voice worth hearing — **the remarks and the relationships are one system doing two jobs**, information and attachment | `DESIGN.md` § There is NO TUTORIAL, `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c, § P2e |
| `@X142` | ⚠⚠ **the helper with NO TASK is the one who speaks** (owner, 2026-08-26) — the player gives the orders, so an unassigned worker reporting in is what a foreman hears, and `DESIGN.md` § 8's management loop supplies its own feedback channel.  ⚠⚠ **It supersedes `@X136`'s progress heuristic and is strictly better**: it reads a task list that already exists rather than six counters and a tuned window, it **cannot misfire on a parked player who is repairing**, and it is a fact the player can see and act on.  ⚠⚠ **It self-calibrates** — assign everyone and there is silence; land with nobody assigned and the whole crew is idle, so `@X135`'s onboarding moment falls out of the rule instead of being special-cased.  ⚠ Idleness picks WHO and WHEN; `@X129`'s skills pick WHAT, so the ex-miner says *"there's ore on that ridge"* rather than *"I am idle"* | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c, `DESIGN.md` § 9, § 11 |
| `@X143` | ⚠ **ONE speaks, never six** — a chorus of idle helpers is the opposite of *not intrusively*; pick the one whose domain best matches where the player is standing.  ⚠ And an idle helper speaks **when there is something new in their domain**, never on a repeat timer, so `@X136`'s silence still governs.  ⚠⚠ **Assigning work is the in-fiction OFF SWITCH**: a player who finds the crew talkative silences them by doing the thing the remarks were nudging toward — the remedy for the hint system is the play it wanted.  ⚠ Busywork-to-buy-quiet is self-limiting, because **helper-seconds are the real bottleneck** (`DESIGN.md` § 13), so a wasted order costs exactly what the player was short of | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2c |
| `@X144` | ⚠⚠ **the DEBRIEF — after a mission the player may optionally ask the crew what they thought** (owner, 2026-08-26).  ⚠ **PULL where `@X129`'s remarks are PUSH**: a nudge you can ignore and a question you can ask, neither of which is a wall.  ⚠⚠ **It is HOW `@X117`'s dominant axis accumulates**, which § P1 never said — *play again and notice* is a weak answer for the axis the design rests on, and this is where a sortie becomes a lesson, diegetically and optionally.  ⚠ Lives at the station (`DESIGN.md` § 16) | `PROGRESSION.md` § P2f |
