But we have something to draw on the planet at the start"*).  ⚠⚠ **It fills a real gap in `@X224`**: *other players are the seed* cannot be the whole story for the FIRST player on a fresh planet, because there is no history yet.  ⚠ **The GIVEN** — terrain plus the initial installation graph — is derived once and is the same for everybody at t = 0; **the ACCUMULATED** — raided POIs, denied routes, abandoned bases, caches (`@X174`) — is written by players, one finished sortie at a time (`@X306`).  ⚠⚠ **And the reason a real-world map is the right GIVEN is that it is AUTHOR-FREE**: nobody designed the Ortler massif, it was imported — so `@X224`'s *"I do not want to know what to find before I boot up the game"* is met **even at t = 0**, by a layer with a history nobody in this project invented.  ***A heightmap is not authored, so importing one buys the anti-optimisation property before a single player has done anything.***  ⚠ It also satisfies `@X244`'s *scale must not be assumed*: **the given layer has to carry the whole game on its own**, because a planet nobody has played yet must already be worth landing on.  ⚠ **And it is CONTENT, so its licence travels with it** — `assets/README.md`'s rule for the one binary the game loads, applied to elevation data with a provenance and a term | `docs/ERRANDS.md`, `@X224`, `@X307`, `@X244`, `@X174` |
| `@X309` | ⚠⚠ **CORRECTION TO `@X307`: `../crawler`'s ORTLER MAP IS A CALIBRATION FIXTURE, NOT THE GAME'S WORLD.**  Inspection found **two** world-map artefacts and only one feeds the game: the game reads a **hand-authored 9×7 array of material codes** (`src/overland.loft:73` `ov_mat_rows()`, 1500 m hexes, `overland_new()` taking no arguments and hard-coding `ov_seed: 7` — *the overland is a constant of the build*), while the Ortler `.bin` (EU-DEM 25 m + OSM, 80×80 at 1.5 km, 882 236 bytes) is read **only** by the viewer and three smoke tests.  ⚠⚠ **What the fixture was FOR is the transferable part**: *"is our 14-kind taxonomy enough … **derive** (not guess) the elevations where terrain types change"*, delivered as **two same-scale drawings — (A) the real data, (B) our model — and the GAP between them is the verdict**.  ⚠⚠ **And the payback was FOUR NUMBERS** — `TREELINE 2150`, `ALPINE_TOP 2500`, `ROCK 2800`, `SNOWLINE 3100` — which are literals in the engine today, worth *"meadow 0.3 ‰ → 5 ‰ (~17×)"*.  ⚠ **The verdict was also a REFUSAL**, which is what makes it worth having: *the 14 kinds SUFFICE*, and *at 1.5 km terrain type is an ELEVATION function, not slope — cliffs are not resolvable at 1.5 km*.  ⚠⚠ **So the pattern is not *import a mountain range* — it is *import one, measure your model against it, keep the numbers*.**  ⚠ And the trap crawler names on itself: *"note where F1 was marked FIXED: in the Python render, **not in the engine**"* — a fixture can be green while the game it was for is unchanged | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X307`, `@X312` |
| `@X310` | ⚠⚠ **THE TERRAIN SCALE NEED NOT BE THE ARCHITECTURE SCALE, AND `@X298`'s MILLION IS A CHOICE** — `crawler/src/scale.loft:26-33` has `M_PER_HEX_STEP = 1.5` and `TERRAIN_COMPRESS = 10.0`, so **a hex is 1.5 m of architecture and 15 m of terrain**, and its surface window of 101×101 hexes is *"a wilderness WINDOW (1.5 km across)"* — **a level IS one overland tile, and the million disappears**.  ⚠ dryopea's 1.5 m is LITERAL: `HEX_DIAMETER` is what a wall is across and `@M050`'s five-hex wall is five hexes.  So the fork is *keep 1.5 m literal and a scenario stays ~1 % of a cell* (`@X298`) or *compress terrain and a scenario becomes a whole cell, at the price of a hill being ten times nearer than it looks*.  ⚠⚠ **`@X312` settles it: a backdrop whose distances LIE is worse than no backdrop**, so 1.5 m stays literal — but it is now a decision rather than an inheritance, and it is the number that decides whether a coarse cell is a neighbourhood or a level | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X298`, `@X312` |
| `@X311` | ⚠⚠ **WHEN A CLASSIFIER CANNOT BE MADE ACCURATE, REPLACE IT WITH A PROCESS WHOSE CONSERVATION CAN BE GATED** — `crawler/plans/2-chunked-lod-world/s6-subhex-finding.md`, and it is a FALSIFICATION rather than a design.  It set out to find a sub-hex geometric signal separating cliff from forest and found none: *"**forest sits on terrain just as steep and rugged as rock**"*, the Alpine boundary being the **treeline** (elevation + exposure, already in the height bands) and *not* geometric; *"`slope > θ → cliff` gives **ZERO κ gain** — best κ +0.464 at θ=50°, **identical to the height-band baseline**"*; so *"no simple geometric sub-hex signal separates cliffs from forest at ~350 m resolution"*.  ⚠⚠ **The resolution was to stop classifying and simulate a PROCESS**: the talus model — bedrock plus rubble, rubble cannot hold a slope past the **angle of repose (~35°)** so it slides to lower neighbours (sandpile relaxation), bedrock past repose strips to **bare face** and the shed rubble piles as **scree**.  ⚠⚠ **And the GATE changed with it, which is the transferable half**: *"**Gate is the INVARIANTS, not κ**: rubble conserved, relaxed surface ≤ repose everywhere, watertight across seams, deterministic."*  ⚠ dryopea already prefers this shape — `plans/22` refuses LOD because *"its gate is 'the outcome is unchanged', which is a much weaker statement"* | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `plans/22` |
| `@X312` | ⚠⚠ **THE SECOND REASON FOR A WORLD MAP IS A BACKDROP OF REAL GEOGRAPHY, AND IT MOVES THE RECOMMENDATION TO REAL DATA** (owner, 2026-08-28) — *"I want to have a backdrop world with real features too, so a far away view for mountains, seas, islands, rivers etc.  Yes there is fog in this world but that doesn't mean that there are no circumstances where a view is possible.  They should feel **earned** by players and feel **impactful**."*  ⚠ The economy (`@X298`) is served by an authored array of material codes; **a backdrop needs a SKYLINE**, and a 9×7 array will not silhouette convincingly at 40 hexes — which is `@X308`'s *a heightmap is not authored* arriving for a second, independent reason.  ⚠⚠ **It is also the cheapest believability in the design by `@X303`'s test**: it simulates NOTHING, nothing walks on it, no rule has to be boundable — a picture of ground the player will never stand on.  ⚠⚠ **THE HAZARD IS MEASURED AND IT ALREADY KILLED A DESIGN**: `@M064` puts the follow camera at 30.96° with a 60° fov, so the top of the frame is **0.96° BELOW the horizon** — the default frame contains **no sky**, which falsified the battleships-as-permit-clock (`@X287`).  ⚠⚠ **The owner's own word is what saves it**: *a permit clock must be CONSULTABLE — always, cheaply, at a glance; a view is EARNED — rare, deliberate, an event.*  The refusal was of a signal the frame does not contain; **an earned view is a moment that brings its own frame**.  ⚠ Three rules follow: the moment must come with the frame (a PLACE whose geometry puts the horizon in view — and `@M064` says the horizon enters at **exactly 30.0°** against 30.96°, so it is one degree away, not a redesign); *earned* means the player DID something (§ 11's *position triggers, not key presses*); and *impactful* means it TELLS you something — `EXPLORATION.md` makes *"players must scout to learn what is beyond the haze"*, so **a view is scouting done from a height**, and it is the one place the coarse map is legible to a player at all | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X307`, `@X308`, `@X310`, `@M064`, `@X287`, `@X303` |
| `@X313` | ⚠⚠ **A HEX'S CONTENT IS A FUNCTION OF ITS SIX NEIGHBOURS — their terrain, their elevation, their WATER FLOW** (owner, 2026-08-28, recalling the original design; **it was not written down anywhere**).  `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` § 2's 2023 notes carry the ingredients — *"general slopes (flats, gradual, hills, mountain)"*, *"water flow, amount of water"* — but not the rule that ties them together, and the rule decides the shape of the whole derivation.  ⚠⚠ **Three things follow.  (1) IT IS WHAT MAKES A 1000× INTERPOLATION POSSIBLE AT ALL**: a single cell has a value and NO GRADIENT — nothing in it says which way is downhill — while six neighbours give **slope, aspect and drainage**, which are the three things every terrain classifier reads.  **(2) THE COARSE MAP STORES FAR LESS THAN IT PRODUCES** — six neighbours × three fields is eighteen numbers and a whole cell's terrain comes out of them — which is what makes it small enough to BE a server state (`@X306`).  **(3) IT MAKES THE DERIVATION WATERTIGHT BY CONSTRUCTION**, if the shared thing is computed the same way from both sides — `crawler` states it as commutativity (*corner shared by 3 hexes = the mean of the 3 centres; edge-third shared by 2 = ⅔·home + ⅓·neighbour, swapped for the other slot*), and hashes the SORTED pair for river crossings so both hexes agree.  ⚠ **Water flow is the field dryopea has never had**: it has the palette's `slope` (read at last by `@X284`) and its `drop` (by `@X282`), and flow is a RELATION rather than a property — `crawler` derives it as pit-fill → steepest descent → accumulation.  ⚠⚠ **THE MEASURED TRAP: a neighbourhood rule OVER-PRODUCES LAKES** — *"model 72 lakes vs OSM 6 water cells (~12×); pit-fill floods local 1.5 km basins"*, VERIFIED and still open — the same shape as *cliffs are not resolvable at 1.5 km*, and it wants `@X311`'s answer.  ⚠ It also revives something `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` recorded as dead: *"sampling png onto hex grid"*, written off as a back-door import tool, is what `@X312`'s backdrop rests on | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X311`, `@X312`, `@X306`, `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` § 2 |
| `@X314` | ⚠⚠ **`../crawler` IS NOT A FOREIGN SOURCE — PARTS OF DRYOPEA'S OWN DESIGN LANDED IN IT, INCLUDING THE WAY THE ORTLER MAP IS FORMED** (owner, 2026-08-28).  ⚠ So its patterns are *this idea, tried* rather than somebody else's, which is why several converge with rulings made here independently — `@X299`'s rule-not-state against `OVERLAND.md`'s *"a pure sampler over the one above — never a tree you descend, never data you store"*, `@X301`'s POIs against its two channels, `@X306`'s snapshot-changed against *"only player deltas persist"*.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE ORTLER MESH IS `@X313` MADE CONCRETE**: each hex is 18 triangles over **19 points** — centre, 6 corners, 12 edge-thirds — and every point but the centre is shared, the corner by **three** hexes (the mean of the three centres) and the edge-third by **two** (⅔ home + ⅓ neighbour).  So a hex's drawn surface is literally a function of itself and its six neighbours, and **the rule and its watertightness are the same property**, which is why it can be gated.  ⚠⚠ **AND THEN THE FIXTURE TESTED IT**: the `.bin` stores **real EU-DEM heights at those 19 points** for the 40×40 subset (14 878 vertices), *"not interpolation … the finest real signal the fixture holds"* — so the rule's answer is measured at 40×40 and derived at 80×80 and the two can be compared.  ⚠⚠ **The verdict: the six-neighbour rule gives you GEOMETRY and does not give you LAND COVER** — at ~350 m spacing no geometric sub-hex signal separates cliff from forest (`@X311`), and `@X313`'s spurious lakes are the same limit on the water side.  **Its output is a shape, not a classification**, which is exactly why `@X311`'s answer was to run a physical process over the shape rather than classify harder | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X313`, `@X311`, `@X309` |
| `@X315` | ⚠⚠ **SCENARIO-SCALE DETAIL HAS THREE SOURCES, AND THE SIZE OF THE THING DECIDES WHICH — the coarse map decides WHERE and never WHAT at the scale you play at** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"the ortler map is in a big scale, we want to use that on a smaller scenario scale too — are there trees, mines, roads, rivers, coast, cliffs?"*).  ⚠ **What the Ortler data actually holds**, read off the Overpass query and the stored legend: **trees YES** (`natural=wood` / `landuse=forest`), **rivers YES** (`waterway=river\|stream`, with the direction from steepest descent on the pit-filled DEM rather than from the OSM geometry), **cliffs FETCHED AND THEN LOST** (`natural=cliff\|bare_rock` are queried and the legend folds them into `rock`, and *cliffs are not resolvable at 1.5 km* is the fixture's own verdict), **coast NO** (the Ortler is inland, 568-3835 m), **mines NO** and **roads NO** — `highway` is absent from the query.  ⚠⚠ So the fixture answers TERRAIN questions and cannot answer INFRASTRUCTURE ones, which is the FIELDS/FEATURES split showing through.  ⚠⚠ **The three sources: FIELDS** (continuous, sampled per fine hex from the coarse neighbourhood — elevation, slope, wetness, a ground kind; coarse only), **FEATURES** (discrete world-coordinate records stamped into whichever window holds them — rivers, roads, and dryopea's POIs; exact at any scale), **PROCESSES** (run at fine scale over the shape the fields gave — cliffs and scree, `@X311`).  ⚠⚠ **THE TEST IS MEASURED: anything smaller than ~350 m cannot be read off the coarse data** — the Ortler mesh's own sub-hex spacing, below which the fixture found no signal.  So coast is 1, rivers and roads and **trees** and mines are 2, cliffs are 3.  ⚠⚠ **FIELD → DENSITY, FEATURE → INSTANCE**, which resolves the tree question exactly: a dryopea stem is **TEN HEXES, 13 m**, so a tree is unambiguously an instance with coordinates, and `OSM_WOOD` on a 1.5 km cell says only *there are trees here, this many*.  ⚠ For dryopea concretely: the palette needs no change, **the FEATURES channel does not exist at all** (four authored marker kinds and no world-coordinate list — a POI is that list), **FLOW is still the missing field** (`@X313`) and two of the six things listed are downstream of it, and cliffs are a process dryopea half-has in `height.loft`'s runtime metres | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X313`, `@X311`, `@X301`, `DESIGN.md` § Trees as terrain |
| `@X316` | ⚠⚠ **THE INHERITED SHAPE: THE BLOCK HOLDS THE DETAIL, THE BIG MAP SAYS WHETHER IT APPLIES** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"crawler inherits the biomes code from ZAngband, where **details are stored inside smaller-scale blocks** where **the bigger map gives the input if they apply locally**"*).  ⚠⚠ **It corrects the direction `@X315` was reading in**: `ov_sample` pushing coarse values DOWN through interpolation, noise and a cascade is real and `@X309` measured its ceiling — **62 % agreement, *the ceiling for elevation-only at 1.5 km***.  The inherited model is the other half and **does not degrade with the scale ratio**.  `crawler/OVERLAND.md:418`: *"per-block seeded plasma fractal **anchored at SHARED CORNER values** + per-terrain **LOOKUP TABLES** (fractal band → grass/tree/bush/rock/water) + overlays … **the anchors sample OUR field stack (the rough structure is followed by construction)** … features rasterize in **authority order**."*  ⚠⚠ **THE COARSE MAP CHOOSES THE TABLE.  THE BLOCK CHOOSES THE CELL.  FEATURES OVERWRITE, IN ORDER.**  A mountain is not interpolated into existence — the coarse map says *this is mountain country* and the block places mountain-country detail at its own scale, so **the million-to-one ratio never has to be crossed by interpolation at all**.  ⚠⚠ **And the anchors are `@X313`, which is what makes it CORRECT**: a block's fractal is pinned at values its neighbours also see so adjacent blocks **cannot** disagree, and because the anchors come from the coarse field stack the block **physically cannot contradict the big map** — not checked, CONSTRUCTED, the same property that makes `@X299` work for mobs and `@X311`'s talus gate on conservation.  ⚠⚠ **THE LAW** (`OVERLAND.md:452`): *"**No feature ever sits ON exact lattice geometry, and every feature is owned by the lattice element all its observers share** — maps CONSUME contracts, never regenerate them"* — the generalisation of `@X314`'s corner rule, and the operative half is that two observers must never each compute the same shared thing.  ⚠ dryopea has been bitten by that class already: `@X285` made `VIEW_PPM` PRIVATE for exactly it.  ⚠ It also adds a fourth thing `@X315` lacked: **the BLOCK**, a unit of content at its own scale that the coarse map merely ADMITS | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X313`, `@X314`, `@X315`, `@X309`, `@X285` |
| `@X317` | ⚠⚠ **LAND IN THE OVERLAP — a base goes where several areas' influence overlaps, BECAUSE THE OVERLAP IS WHAT MAKES A CHOICE EXIST** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"we can work with a limited set of interesting areas that get used in the scenario, preferably with putting the player in the overlap region of several of them **to give them a choice**"*).  ⚠ It closes `@X305` from the other end: that one says *two to four POIs, each load-bearing, and the count should exceed what one sortie can act on*; this says **where the base goes relative to them**.  ⚠⚠ **One area in reach is not a decision, it is a task list** — several in reach with time for fewer than all of them is `@X197`'s assignment pillar read at the SORTIE scale.  ⚠⚠ **And it needs no new geometry, which is the sign it is right**: `@X300` already gives every POI a static bound indexed once for the sortie, so *landing in the overlap* is exactly **the base's window intersecting several bounds at once** — the same number that culls a population decides whether a landing site is any good.  ⚠ **The unit of influence is a TRIP, not a radius**: the bubble is 25 hexes = 32.5 m and a sortie drives much further, so *in play* means *there and back inside the sortie*.  ⚠⚠ **It gives `DESIGN.md` § 15's landing pick a meaning it does not have** — *click ANY hex* becomes *choose which overlap to sit in*, which is `@X019`'s *the base layout is the exam* one level up: **the exam starts before you land**.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE ANTI-PATTERN IS MEASURED**: `@M058` is a base sealed so completely it *"still stands at 378"* with thirteen robots alive and **zero targets** — *a wave that cannot reach you cannot die, and salvage is the only income* — so **a landing site with no overlap is that outcome by geography instead of by masonry**, a stalemate the player cannot even lose.  ⚠ **The gate**: a landing site earns its place if **moving the base OUT of the overlap changes which POI the player deals with** — a scenario pair whose one token is the landing hex | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X305`, `@X300`, `@X197`, `@X019`, `@M058`, `DESIGN.md` § 15 |
| `@X318` | ⚠⚠ **THE WORKED EXAMPLE — a steep mountain side admits a LIMITED SET of blocks, and a block DECLARES WHAT IT NEEDS RATHER THAN WHERE IT GOES** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"on a steep mountain side we have a limited set of blocks that are possible — with rock faces on them and with steep terrain — **some of these blocks hold a river or a waterfall**"*).  ⚠⚠ It is `@X316`'s *the bigger map gives the input if they apply locally* made operational: the coarse cell does not describe the ground, **it supplies PREDICATES that admit blocks** — the elevation band admits the alpine/rock set, the **slope** (which only the six neighbours can give, `@X313`) admits blocks carrying rock faces, **flow present** admits the subset that holds a river, and **flow plus a big drop along it** admits the subset holding a **waterfall**.  ⚠ So *steep mountain side* is not one answer but a small admissible set, and which member lands is the block's own seed.  ⚠⚠ **A BLOCK IS A ROW — conditions on one side, contents on the other, and no placement code anywhere** — the trait seam this ecosystem already uses twice: `crawler/src/monsters.loft` (*"the engine never asks for a monster BY KEY — it asks the merged catalog for one that FITS A PLACE"*) and `ROBOT_ECONOMY.md`'s *an installation costs one ROW*.  ⚠⚠ **AND DRYOPEA'S PALETTE IS ALREADY THE OUTPUT VOCABULARY**: the example needs `rock` (slope 20, `@X284`'s sturdiest footing), `steep_rock` (slope 40, `walk_ground: false` — the cliff, and `@X286` corrected it to refuse vehicles too), `rapids` (drop 3) and `waterfall` (drop 8) — **all four since plan 01, with the numbers already priced against the 3.0 m boost**, so a boost just leaves rapids and *"a waterfall is a hole nothing gets out of"*.  ⚠⚠ **So a waterfall block is terrain with real teeth and nothing has ever placed one** — `@X282` and `@X286` were both reasoning about a player-dug trench, and the same numbers make an authored waterfall a hazard the player cannot undo.  ⚠⚠ **THE STITCHING RULE: a river must ENTER and LEAVE where its neighbours' rivers do**, so a river-bearing block is indexed by its conditions AND by its entry/exit edge pair — and the coarse **flow direction is exactly what picks that pair**, which makes the catalogue an EDGE-MATCHING set: elaborate content that still stitches, because the matching is done by a field both neighbours read rather than by a check afterwards.  ⚠ A waterfall is then the block whose outgoing edge drops further than a block can absorb — one row, not a rule.  ⚠⚠ **And it answers `@X313`'s measured trap**: a block catalogue never invents standing water, so it cannot over-produce lakes the way a pit-fill does (72 against 6) | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X316`, `@X313`, `@X286`, `@X284`, `@X282` |
| `@X319` | ⚠⚠ **EVERY COMMON CASE NEEDS SEVERAL BLOCKS, AND THE AUTHORING BUDGET BELONGS TO THE DULL ONES** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"I want a choice of multiple blocks for **all common cases** so we can **dither** the terrain even when the world-map is not very interesting, so we prevent the common problem where **patterns get visible**"*).  ⚠⚠ **It inverts the instinct**: the tempting spend is the dramatic case — the waterfall, the gorge — but those appear rarely and **repetition is only visible in what appears OFTEN**.  *A gorge seen once is a gorge; a grass shelf seen forty times is a pattern.*  ⚠ **The honest arithmetic if a block were a STAMP** is a birthday problem, `n ≈ k(k−1)/2 ln 2`, and at dryopea's 40-hex haze (104 m visible): a 10-hex block gives ~64 co-visible and wants **2 908** variants, a 20-hex ~16 and **173**, a 41-hex ~4 and **9** — so **block SIZE is the lever and it trades quadratically**, with a hex lattice giving 6 rotations × 2 reflections free.  ⚠⚠ **But `@X316` already refuses the stamp reading**: *per-block seeded plasma fractal anchored at shared corner values* means **a block is a RULE APPLIED TO ANCHORS**, so two instances on different corner heights are already different ground — what can repeat is the ARRANGEMENT, not the geometry, and the real budget is a handful of variants × 12 orientations over continuously varying anchors.  It is the property `@X299` relies on for mobs: the thing is generated from its context, so context varies it for free.  ⚠⚠ **And the dither must be a POSITION HASH, never a stream**: dryopea gates 16 goldens, `mesh_crc` and 920 measurements, so terrain must be reproducible — and **crawler has exactly this bug undocumented**, its `gseed` passing literal `1, 1` where the window coordinates belong, so every window draws the identical sequence | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X316`, `@X318`, `@X299` |
| `@X320` | ⚠⚠ **A BLOCK'S EDGES ARE NOT ITS TO VARY — the edge band is owned by the EDGE and both neighbours contribute, which is what makes variants SUBSTITUTABLE** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"the side of blocks get features from multiple blocks to prevent visible seams"*).  ⚠ It is `@X316`'s ownership law carried from HEIGHTS to FEATURES: *every feature is owned by the lattice element all its observers share — maps CONSUME contracts, never regenerate them*.  So a block's INTERIOR is its own and **is the only part a variant may vary**; the EDGE BAND belongs to the edge and is read by both blocks; a CORNER belongs to the corner and is read by three.  ⚠⚠ **Read it as the REASON rather than the technique**: if an edge band belonged to one block, swapping that block for a variant would move the seam and **break its neighbour** — so variants could not be substituted and `@X319`'s dither would be impossible.  Because the edge owns its band, **every member of an admissible set is interchangeable by construction**.  ⚠ It is emphatically NOT *blend the two afterwards*, which is a second computation of a shared thing and is what the law forbids — the same hazard `@X285` made `VIEW_PPM` private to prevent.  **One owner, both neighbours reading**, commutative for free and needing no check.  ⚠ And it compounds with the dither: a seam carrying features from both sides does not announce where one block ended, so the ARRANGEMENT blurs as well as the geometry | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X316`, `@X319`, `@X285` |
| `@X321` | ⚠⚠ **A BLOCK CONTRIBUTES A RESIDUAL, NEVER AN ABSOLUTE — the detail is ADDED to the coarse height field** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"combine the inner block details like rock-faces with the heights provided by the 1.5 km map and we get **more interesting and realistic shapes**"*).  `height(fine hex) = coarse_field(from the six neighbours) + block_residual(this block, its seed)`.  ⚠ `@X316` states half — *the anchors sample OUR field stack, the rough structure is followed by construction* — and `crawler` names the other half in one word: *"**the plasma residual** is the per-type micro-roughness number"*.  ⚠⚠ **It is why the shapes read as real**: real ground IS a large form with local structure on it, and neither layer alone produces that — the coarse field alone is a smooth ramp (`@X309`'s measured 62 % ceiling) and the block alone is a face floating on nothing, identical everywhere.  ⚠⚠ **AND IT IS THE BEST ANSWER TO `@X319`'s PATTERN PROBLEM: the same block on a different slope is a different landform** — a rock-face row on a gentle grade is a low outcrop, the identical row on a 40° flank is a cliff band.  One row, many appearances, free — so the variant budget is multiplied by however much the coarse field varies underneath, which is continuous, and **that rather than the variant count is what actually defeats visible tiling**; `@X319`'s birthday arithmetic is the pessimistic bound, not the requirement.  ⚠ **It satisfies `@X320`'s edge contract by CONSTRUCTION**: a residual tapering to zero at the edges leaves the coarse field untouched there, so neighbours agree without either knowing about the other — the property is built in, nothing to check.  ⚠⚠ **The honest limit: a residual may not FIGHT the field** — thirty metres of face on valley floor is a mountain in a meadow — **which is exactly why `@X318` has a block DECLARE WHAT IT NEEDS**: the conditions are not a filing system, they are the guarantee that the residual lands on ground it makes sense on (`crawler`'s frozen intent: *"rivers run the low ground … no water on ridgelines"*).  ⚠⚠ **And dryopea already composes exactly this way one scale down**: `height.loft` is a runtime layer of metres over an authored ground and `hex_height` is the SUM, read by `@X284`'s footing, `@X282`'s waterline and every passability check — so this is *rubble is a LAYER, never a repaint* at 1.5 km, and `@X311`'s talus is its process form (bedrock plus rubble, gated on the rubble being conserved) | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X316`, `@X318`, `@X319`, `@X320`, `@X311`, `@X284`, `@X282` |
| `@X322` | ⚠⚠ **THIS IS LIBRARY WORK — THREE LAYERS, AND THE LIBRARY NEVER ASKS FOR A THING BY NAME** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"most of the routines we write here should be **generic enough to be implemented by multiple games**, so I want **libraries with their implementations** and **possible scripts for edge cases that can be provided by individual games**"*).  ⚠ It is `CLAUDE.md` § Loft consumer relationship pointed the other way, which is what `plans/10` exists for.  ⚠⚠ **The split: (1) the LIBRARY** owns the mechanism with no policy in it — the six-neighbour derivation (`@X313`), the ownership law (`@X316`), residual composition (`@X321`), the bound and the CULL/EVALUATE/MATERIALISE tiers (`@X300`), edge-matching (`@X318`), the closed form and `slip` (`@X302`); **(2) the CATALOGUE** is the game's, declaratively — palette, block rows, POI kinds, errand roles; **(3) the SCRIPT** is the game's, imperatively — `.keys` here, `bundles/` in `crawler`.  ⚠⚠ ***The library never asks for a thing BY NAME.  It asks the catalogue for one that FITS.***  ⚠ That sentence is `crawler/src/monsters.loft:26` verbatim, `ROBOT_ECONOMY.md`'s *an installation costs one ROW*, and `@X318`'s *a block declares what it needs, never where it goes* — **three systems, one seam, arrived at independently**.  ⚠ Layer 3 exists in both repos already: `crawler` runs an authored overlay the engine executes (*"the active overlay decides; we stamp"*) and dryopea has `script.loft` and 50 `.keys`.  **So the seam is: the library reads a catalogue and a script may OVERRIDE in authority order** — the library decides HOW an override composes, the game decides WHAT overrides.  ⚠⚠ **BUT EXTRACT ON THE SECOND CONSUMER, NOT THE FIRST**: `plans/10`'s bar is *extract what SURVIVES*, and **a library with one consumer is a refactor with a version number**.  The second consumer is real and present — `crawler` has the ZAngband micro layer, the Ortler pipeline, the trimesh and the hydrology built, and `@X314` says parts are dryopea's design to begin with — so **build the seams as library seams from day one, land the first implementation where it is cheapest to gate, and publish when the second consumer has read the API and disagreed with it**.  ⚠ Naming is `plans/10`'s: **descriptive, never a brand** — no `moros_*` and no `dryopea_*` — and `hex_terrain` / `hex_world` are what to read before proposing a new package.  ⚠⚠ **And what is NOT library work is every number and every refusal**: `@X303`'s test, `@X305`'s two-to-four, `@X317`'s overlap, `@X312`'s earned view, and *a distraction must be caused by something the player BUILT* — **the library supplies the shape; the game supplies the reasons**, and a library enforcing *two to four POIs* would be a game wearing a library's name | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `docs/ERRANDS.md`, `plans/10`, `@X318`, `@X314` |
| `@X323` | ⚠⚠ **THE THESIS: EXPRESS DETAIL FROM A VERY COMPACT BASE SET — and it is a REFUSAL TEST** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"it all comes down to a way to express detail based on a very compact base set of data"*).  ⚠ Every worldgen and errand ruling is an answer to it, and the family is worth reading at once: `@X313` **eighteen numbers** give slope, aspect and drainage a cell alone cannot have; `@X316` a **row and a seed** — the coarse map picks the table, the block picks the cell; `@X318` conditions are a **predicate**, so placement is never stored; `@X321` **one row** becomes many landforms because the same block on a different slope is a different one; `@X319` 6 rotations × 2 reflections are **free**; `@X320` costs **nothing at all** — it is a restriction that buys substitutability; `@X299` **five anchors** give a mob its whole life at any `t`; `@X302` **one integer** keeps the closed form under deviation; `@X300` **one region per POI** turns a scan into a query; `@X306` **the snapshot changed** makes a campaign out of a few rows; `@X308` a **heightmap nobody authored** gives a world the author does not know at t = 0.  ⚠⚠ ***Every one is a COMPRESSION with a rule for decompressing it, and every rule is LOCAL, DETERMINISTIC and COMMUTATIVE*** — local so a piece needs no neighbour produced first, deterministic so it can be thrown away and recomputed, commutative so two owners of a shared thing agree without talking.  **Take one away and the compression stops being usable**, which is why `@X316`'s anchors, `@X320`'s edge ownership and `@X319`'s position hash keep turning out to be one requirement in different hats.  ⚠⚠ **SO THE TEST IS: DOES THIS ADD DATA IN PROPORTION TO THE DETAIL IT PRODUCES?**  If it does it is the wrong mechanism — refusing stored fine terrain, a block as a stamp (`@X319` priced it at **2 908** variants), per-cell node inventories, a stored per-mob path, a bespoke result format, and a bigger palette (`@X309` measured that one: *the 14 kinds SUFFICE*).  ⚠⚠ **AND DRYOPEA HAS DONE THIS SINCE PLAN 01 WITHOUT NAMING IT**: `painted.loft` is sparse and sea-default so **absence IS the sea**; `height.loft` is *a LAYER, never a repaint*; `part.loft` derives every entity's geometry from a tree; `pose.loft` reads the sim rather than a second flag; `entity_view.loft` holds **no state at all**; `carry.loft` makes conservation structural with one owner field; `mesh_chunks.loft` is the painted set plus a ring.  **So `@X321` at 1.5 km is not an analogy — it is the same rule, and the repo has seven other instances of it** | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `docs/ERRANDS.md`, `@X313`, `@X316`, `@X319`, `@X321`, `@X299`, `@X306`, `@X303` |
| `@X324` | ⚠⚠ **IT IS AN OLD DESIGN, AIMED AT A GAP — WHICH IS WHAT MAKES ITS PIECES NON-OPTIONAL** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"that is why it is an old design — I am already designing this for a few years, because it **hides a gap found in all modern games I observe**"*).  ⚠ **The repo carries the evidence of the age**: `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` § 2 is the 2023 `todo` file and `@X313`'s six-neighbour rule is already in it in its original form — *"sampling png onto hex grid: **specific point & 4 pixels around it**"* — beside *"general slopes"* and *"water flow, amount of water"*.  So it is at minimum a three-year-old design in writing in this repository, **and the coherence of the parts is because they were fitted rather than collected**.  ⚠⚠ **The gap, as the design's own rulings state it** (the owner named the symptom; this is the reading assembled from `@X303`, `@X323`, `@X319`, `@X321`, `@X308`, `@X224`): **AUTHORED content is specific and believable and MEANT — and finite, and KNOWN, by the author first and the player soon after; GENERATED content is endless and cheap and surprising to the author — and shallow, PATTERNED, and meaning nothing in particular.**  ***The gap is that nobody gets BELIEVABLE DETAIL AT SCALE***, and this architecture is the claim that a compact base set plus local decompression rules gets a world that is neither known nor shallow.  ⚠ `@X224` is the sharpest existing statement and it is the author complaining about their OWN game — *if the seed is a number the author chose then the author knows the world* — **the gap seen from inside**, with `@X308`'s author-free heightmap as one answer.  ⚠⚠ **THREE CONSEQUENCES FOR HOW THE DESIGN IS TREATED. (1) A piece dropped for convenience is a REGRESSION even when everything still works**: `@X320`'s edge restriction buys nothing visible alone and its absence makes `@X319`'s dither impossible; `@X321`'s residual looks like an implementation detail and its absence takes the variant budget from a handful to thousands.  **(2) The design is not finished when it RUNS** — `@X303` and `@X323` are tests rather than features, and a version passing neither while producing terrain has produced the thing the gap is about.  **(3) It is why `@X322`'s library split matters more than usual**: a mechanism aimed at a gap is worth more than one game, and the several years are what make it plausible the seams are in the right places.  ⚠ It also explains why `crawler`'s independently-built machinery keeps agreeing with rulings made here — `@X314` gave the reason: **it is the same design, in two places** | `docs/WORLDGEN.md`, `@X323`, `@X303`, `@X224`, `@X308`, `@X314`, `@X322`, `DESIGN_HISTORY.md` § 2 |

It must be the cycle's reach **dilated by the deviation**, which is only possible if the deviation is BOUNDED — and nothing today bounds it, so a probe now gates the phase.  ⚠ **(2) *materialising at `R` and at `2R` gives identical positions*** — false wherever anything can deviate a body, because the `2R` copy has been a body longer and deviated more.  The claim that survives is narrower — *identical where nothing can deviate a body; and where something can, they differ by exactly `slip`* — which **recovers `@X299` rather than weakening it**, because two un-materialised mobs cannot block each other.  ⚠⚠ **Except for terrain, which is everywhere** — so *if the cycle is not terrain-aware the equality fails everywhere*, and that raised an existing probe from a cost question to a design one.  ⚠⚠ **Both were invisible to re-reading**: the prose was internally consistent, and only asking *what would falsify this* moved them.  ⚠ It also produced the plan's missing first section — the invariant stated as ONE sentence, and its **twelve** silent re-assertion sites counted before a line is written, with the two cures the repo already keeps six times over (ONE door; make omission loud) | `plans/30`, `@X299`, `@X300`, `@X302` |
| `@X327` | ⚠⚠ **FORMAL RULES: `@FR-<Name>`, A THIRD FAMILY AND A THIRD TENSE — and a THIRD TIER that GENERATES** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"for all the systems we make I want **formal rules that describe them including their invariants**"* and *"create a **formal system that generates** this world and the AI rules"*).  [`docs/FORMAL.md`](../docs/FORMAL.md), read against `../loft`, which has run this for months.  ⚠⚠ **dryopea's three existing families are all HISTORY**: `@X` is a decision (past — it happened once), `@M` a measurement (past — on a day, on a binary), `@D` a defect (past, and fixed).  **A RULE is none of those** — it claims something about every case including the ones nobody has run, and loft states the doctrine: ***the rules do not change to match the code; the code changes to match the rules***.  ⚠ So some `@X` rows are rules wearing a decision's clothes and should be PROMOTED rather than duplicated (`@X296` → `@FR-E-Order-Wins`, `@X313` → `@FR-W-Neighbourhood`, `@X321` → `@FR-W-Residual`), the `@X` keeping the *who decided and against what alternative* half.  ⚠⚠ **THREE TIERS, and the third is dryopea's addition to loft's system**: **PROSE** (a statement a person reads and a tool only resolves), **CHECKED** (plus a predicate a tool evaluates — `@X325`, loft's `o_proxy_check.py`), and **GENERATIVE** (plus a machine form **the running game reads**).  ***At tier 3 the rule is not checked against the code — the rule IS the code's input***, which is what *a formal system that generates this world* means and is `@X322`'s catalogue layer given a spine.  ⚠⚠ **AND DRYOPEA ALREADY HAS A TIER-3 CATALOGUE THAT NOTHING READS**: `examples/numbers.json` holds **353 leaf numbers** already shaped `{value, units, doc}` and no code path loads it, while 23 `.loft` sites restate values by hand.  ⚠ A drift probe found **zero drift and four false positives, all the probe's own** — because the relation between a catalogue value and its constant takes at least **four unstated shapes**: same-line citation, next-line citation, a **unit conversion** (2.0 s → 6 000 000 base units), a **derived unit** (10 points per 0.5 m body → 20 per metre) and a **wildcard family**.  ***A checker cannot verify what the comment does not say***, so the first tier-3 job is not *assert equality* but **make the relation machine-readable** — and the four shapes are `= v`, `= v × K`, `= v ÷ w`, `family` | `docs/FORMAL.md`, `@X322`, `@X323`, `@X325`, `../loft/doc/claude/formal/` |
| `@X328` | ⚠⚠ **THE EIGHT LESSONS CARRIED FROM `../loft`'s FORMAL SYSTEM, AND EVERY ONE IS A FAILURE IT HAD** — the reason to copy the system rather than invent one ([`docs/FORMAL.md`](../docs/FORMAL.md) § The lessons carried).  ⚠⚠ **1. *OPEN: 0* is a claim, not a fact, and it failed FOUR independent ways** — every time a conformance corpus **held an axis fixed**: an all-`(integer, integer)` oracle missing two `text` deviations; a corpus sweeping four axes while *"holding the ARGUMENT SPELLING fixed at a variable in every cell"*, whose one moved axis found **six more** bugs; a corpus whose held-fixed axis *"was not the subject at all but the MOMENT"*; and *"the THIRD doc whose zero rested on an all-scalar corpus"*.  ***A corpus that varies the subject exhaustively still proves nothing about the axis it never varies — count what is held FIXED, not what is swept.***  ⚠⚠ **dryopea has that hazard already named three times** — *a 1-hex corridor cannot tell a flow field from a fixed heading*, *a world where every source hex is at 0 m cannot tell a RISE from a destination height*, *the instrument is a CROSS-PRODUCT* — found one at a time, which is what a rules file turns into one question.  ⚠ **2. Single-sourcing makes the answers agree; it does not make anyone ASK.**  **3. A shared list is not automatically one rule** — four of eight candidate families SPLIT rather than merged, *"each split a merge that would have coupled two rules that must stay free to differ"*.  ⚠⚠ **4. A number from an instrument that cannot represent what it counts is not a measurement** — loft's rule count moved 361 → 356 → 251 → 268 → 285 as *"the instrument learned what it was counting"*, and **dryopea reproduced it TWICE in one day**: `tags.sh` reporting four dangling tags it could not read as a RANGE row, and the `numbers.json` probe reporting four drifts that were all its own parsing.  **Both instruments were wrong before the tree was.**  ⚠ **5. A diagnostic GATES iff ignoring it can produce a WRONG RESULT** — the whole blocking/advisory split.  **6. A gate that examined NOTHING must not read as a pass.**  **7. Prose does not fire at the moment you act.**  **8. The index is GENERATED from the citations, never kept beside them** — *"a second copy of where the rules live is the defect this convention exists to remove"*.  ⚠⚠ **And the one that governs adoption** (`BUG_REVIEW.md:305`): ***the remedy is NOT to add citations*** — *"a tree at 76 cited → 255 cited with the same duplication underneath would read as progress while nothing had changed … evaluate the sites → de-duplicate onto one home → fix what the disagreement was already causing → then cite.  **The citation is the RECEIPT, not the task.**"* | `docs/FORMAL.md`, `@X327`, `@X325`, `CLAUDE.md` § Testing something that moves |
| `@X329` | ⚠⚠ **READING A CITATION COUNT: TWO SITES MAY ENFORCE ONE RULE ONLY IF THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THEM CAN BE NAMED** (owner, 2026-08-28: *"if there are multiple implementations of the same rule they should be **different in a way**; if they are **completely different** the rule is probably **too broad**, but if they are **too similar** you probably want a **single implementation** (possibly with a flag to indicate differences)"*).  ⚠⚠ **That is what `rules.sh sites @FR-X` returning N > 1 is FOR** — the count alone says nothing, and the reading is three-way with the MIDDLE band the healthy one: sites differing **completely** mean the RULE is too broad and **splits**; sites differing in a **NAMEABLE** way are healthy and the difference gets written down; sites barely differing at all mean **one implementation, with a FLAG carrying the difference as data**.  ⚠⚠ ***The test is whether the difference can be NAMED, and whether the name is a fact about the DOMAIN rather than about the code.***  ⚠ It is the design protocol's two failure modes read off a NUMBER instead of off a feeling — under-unify is N mechanisms for one family, over-unify is one mechanism forced over N families, and this tells them apart.  ⚠⚠ **And *with a flag* is dryopea's own pattern five times over**: `can_climb` takes a CLIMB and not a kind, one mover serves four robot roles off a class row, `salvage_at` is *the shared chassis*, `crew_work_units` is *the ONE door for build and repair alike*, `place_marker` is *the ONE dispatch* — **a healthy merge replaces a difference in CODE with a difference in DATA**.  ⚠ **The counter-force, measured by loft**: *a shared list is not automatically one rule* — four of eight candidate families SPLIT, *"each split a merge that would have coupled two rules that must stay free to differ"* — so the test is about naming the QUESTION, never about comparing the text.  ***Equality is EVIDENCE; sameness-of-rule is the CLAIM***, and a `@FR-` citation is that claim written down.  ⚠ Shipped as `scripts/rules.sh --review`, a REPORT and never a gate | `docs/FORMAL.md`, `@X327`, `@X328`, `@FR-F-Nameable-Difference` |

| `@X331` | ⚠⚠ **A MOB'S DISTANCE TO ITS DESTINATION NEVER INCREASES, WHICH IS WHAT BOUNDS A DEVIATING BODY — `plans/30` R0 probe 3, ANSWERED BY READING.**  `@X326` found R5's gate false as written (*the bound contains the cycle* — a sidestep puts a body on a hex the cycle never visits), so the bound had to be the cycle **dilated by the deviation**, which is only possible if the deviation is bounded.  ⚠ Nothing bounded it, and the phase was blocked.  ⚠⚠ **The answer was already in the code, in the difference between two functions**: `flow_steps` (`flow.loft:476`) admits a neighbour only when `flow_distance(n) < d` — **strictly closer** — and `flow_sidesteps` (`:514`) only when `== d` — **exactly equal**.  So a step decreases the distance, a sidestep holds it, and **nothing increases it**.  ⚠⚠ **THE BOUND FALLS OUT WITH NO CAP NEEDED**: a leg starting at distance `L` keeps the body inside `disc(destination, L)` for its whole duration, deviating or not, so ***the dilated bound is the union over legs of the disc centred on each anchor with the incoming leg's length as its radius*** — static, computable from the five anchors before the mob moves, and it provably CONTAINS a deviating body.  ⚠ **It is wider than the cycle's path and that is honest**: a queued mob can circle an iso-distance ring, so the deviation is **bounded in SPACE and not in TIME**, and the time is what `slip` absorbs.  ⚠⚠ **The caveat is the transferable half**: this holds *because* the sidestep is equal-distance-only, so **an errand mover's sidestep must be written the same way** — one admitting a FURTHER neighbour looks harmless and silently breaks R5's bound and R6's equality, which is `@X324`'s *a piece dropped for convenience is a regression even when everything still works*.  ⚠ Written down as `@FR-E-Non-Increasing` and cited at `flow_sidesteps`, so the next person to widen it is told what it costs | `plans/30`, `@X326`, `@X300`, `@X324`, `@FR-E-Non-Increasing` |
| `@X332` | ⚠⚠ **THE CREW'S JOB RECORD GENERALISES TO A MOB; THE CREW'S SELECTION RULE DOES NOT — `@FR-F-Nameable-Difference`'s first real application.**  `plans/30` R0 probe 2, answered by reading `task.loft`'s surface.  ⚠ **SHARE**: `Job { found, kind, q, r }` (a leg's destination is exactly a kind and a hex — no nameable difference) and `TASK_*` as the **arrival action** vocabulary (*what you do when you get there* is the same four things).  ⚠ **DO NOT SHARE**: `jobs_in_scope`, which takes a `Skills` and searches near a point — **a mob has no skills and does not search** — and `job_pick`, whose nearest-wins is a CHOICE a mob does not have.  ⚠⚠ ***The crew CHOOSE what to do from where they stand; a mob is TOLD where to go and does what the place needs on arrival.***  That difference is nameable and a fact about the domain, so the two selection rules **stay apart** — which is loft's *four of eight families split rather than merged* landing here on its first application.  ⚠⚠ **And a ROLE is not a job KIND**: a role selects a CYCLE, a kind is the ACTION on arrival, so **they compose rather than merge** — which is why sharing the vocabulary costs nothing and merging the selection would have coupled two rules that must stay free to differ | `plans/30`, `@X329`, `@FR-F-Nameable-Difference` |
| `@X333` | ⚠⚠ **A ROLE IS A ROW AND THE TABLE IS INDEXED, NEVER COMPARED — AND A TEST SWEEPS `src/` TO SAY SO.**  `plans/30` R1, `src/errand.loft` § A ROLE IS A ROW.  ⚠ `docs/ERRANDS.md` § Roles are a TABLE and `@X322` both asked for it; what is new is the ENFORCEMENT, because the failure it prevents is one somebody has already had: `../crawler` compares `role == 7` in **eight** places and its own `src/sim.loft:145` records the bill — *"the role list stopped at 6 while 7-12 were in use, which costs a reader real time."*  ⚠⚠ **A comparison is not a thing a compiler can refuse**, so `tests/30_r1_the_errand.loft` reads every `src/*.loft` back and fails on a code line comparing a `ROLE_` row constant — with `ROLE_KIND_COUNT` the one exemption, because the table's SIZE is not a role.  ⚠ The gate carries its own control (it must catch a planted comparison and must NOT call the size check one), which is `CLAUDE.md` § a gate that reads PERFECT is as suspect as one that reads wrong.  ⚠⚠ **And the columns are four because `errand_leg` reads four**: *what draws a role off its route* is R7's and is deliberately NOT a column yet — four of crawler's eleven monster flags are declared in its table and read by no AI code at all, which is `@X112`'s *check the number exists first* from the other end | `plans/30`, `@X322`, `@X112`, `@FR-E-Bag-Steers` |
| `@X334` | ⚠⚠ **A MOB'S BAG IS NOT `carry.loft`'s LEDGER, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS NAMEABLE — `@FR-F-Nameable-Difference`'s second application.**  `plans/30` R1.  `CLAUDE.md` says *never a "carried" field on the carrier as well*, and `Errand.carry` is exactly that, so it owes the three-way test an answer.  ⚠ It is: ***`carry.loft` conserves an object that is ON THE MAP and that the player could pick up instead; a mob's bag holds material that was never on the map and never will be.***  A `CarryObject` has an `owner` and conservation is structural because a tower top exists whether or not anybody holds it; a hauler's load comes from off-map and leaves with the hauler, so there is no object to conserve and no second copy to disagree with.  ⚠⚠ **R7 is where that stops being true, and the obligation is recorded now so it is not discovered there**: the moment a mob picks up *the player's salvage heap* it is holding a thing the player could have had, and that load must be a `carry.loft` object with the bag as the routine's READING of it — never a second ledger | `plans/30`, `@X329`, `@FR-F-Nameable-Difference` |
| `@X335` | ⚠⚠ **A CYCLE'S PERIOD IS A COUNT OF HEXES WHEN THE BAG STEERS AND A SPAN OF TIME WHEN A CLOCK DOES — AND THE BANK DOES NOT RESTART AT A LEG BOUNDARY.**  `plans/30` R2, `src/errand.loft` § THE CYCLE.  ⚠ `@FR-E-Closed-Form` asked for *evaluable at an arbitrary `t` in O(legs)*, and the closed form splits in two: **TIME → STEPS is exact arithmetic** — `fixstep`'s `Bank` keeps its remainder, so the hexes released by `t` are `floor(rate × t / BANK_WHOLE)` however `t` was spent — while **STEPS → HEX is a PATH**, which is `@M071` cashed in: a leg is a descent of a flow field and the round is stored as the hexes themselves, one per step offset.  ⚠⚠ **The subtle half is the leg boundary**: a mob that turns at an anchor carries its banked fraction across, so *how far into this leg am I* is `walked(t) − walked(t₀)` and **never** `walked(t − t₀)` — the two differ by a whole hex whenever the carry is non-zero and nothing about the wrong form looks wrong.  ⚠ **It is equally an obligation on R3's mover**: a mob that walks past an anchor spends its remaining hexes on the NEXT leg and never loses them at the turn, and ***a DWELL at an anchor is a LEG of the cycle with a length, not a pause beside it*** — a pause is a second time source and a closed form has room for exactly one.  ⚠⚠ **And the two steerings are ONE function reading ONE column** (`@X329`), the same column `errand_leg` reads: `period == 0` is the bag and the phase is a count of hexes that closes at any distance; `period > 0` is the clock, which picks the leg while the walk only says how far into it the mob got.  ⚠ **A clock period must be a whole number of TICKS and `cycle_fault` refuses one that is not** — `@M074` is what it costs — which is the same *refuse at construction rather than wrap silently* the phase's named control asks for.  ⚠ The round is a FLAT vector of hexes and not a `vector<CycleLeg>` carrying a `vector<Hex>` each, because [loft#974] reads a record's vector field as EMPTY through an accessor and that is the shape exactly | `plans/30`, `@M071`, `@M074`, `@X299`, `@X329`, `@FR-E-Closed-Form`, `@FR-E-Bag-Steers` |
| `@X336` | ⚠⚠ **A MOB'S POSITION IS EXACT IN ITS *PHASE* AND ONLY CONDITIONALLY IN ITS *HEX* — `plans/30`'s ONE-SENTENCE INVARIANT WAS READ TOO STRONGLY.**  `plans/30` R3 + Rc, `src/errand.loft` § THE MOVER, `tests/30_rc_the_conformance.loft`.  ⚠ The plan and § Rc both say *every mob on a cycle satisfies `cycle_at(e, now) == (e.q, e.r)`*, and building the mover falsified it: **a mob whose first choice is taken by a companion walks a DIFFERENT route of the same length and has lost no time at all**, so it is neither on the rule's hex nor late, and `slip` has nothing to record.  ⚠⚠ **`@FR-E-Slip` already said so**: *"a body pushed off its cycle **re-converges on the same hex**"* is a claim about where it ENDS UP — the anchor — and not about where it stands on the way.  ⚠⚠ **What is TOTAL is the DISTANCE**: a mob's field distance to its current anchor equals the rule's, under every deviation, at every moment — an ordinary step decrements both, a sidestep holds both, and a lost hex is paid in `slip` so the rule holds too.  **Hex equality is the special case of it where nothing blocked**, and the corpus measures both: **0 hexes, 0 phases and 0 legs of 320 mob-ticks** in a world with nothing to push a body, against **37 hex and 0 phase** disagreements once three mobs share one routine.  ⚠ The currency costs nothing, because `@FR-E-Boundable` and `@X331` are already stated in field distance — so R5's bound is untouched.  ⚠⚠ **And it is R6's own correction arriving three phases early**: *identical where nothing can deviate a body; and where something can, they differ by exactly `slip`* is this sentence about materialisation radii instead of about a rule and a body, so R6 inherits the reading rather than rediscovering it | 2026-08-28 | `plans/30`, `@M075`, `@X331`, `@FR-E-Slip`, `@FR-E-Boundable` |
| `@X337` | ⚠⚠ **A DWELL IS NOT A BLOCK, AND A CONFORMANCE GATE STRUCTURALLY CANNOT TELL THEM APART — SO LIVENESS IS A SECOND GATE.**  `plans/30` R3, `src/errand.loft::errand_hex`, `tests/30_rc_the_conformance.loft`.  ⚠ The mover charges `slip` for every released hex a mob could not spend on progress, which is right for a blocked one and **wrong for a guard standing at its post**: `cycle_phase` CLAMPS a clock leg's offset at that leg's own length, so those hexes are ones the RULE does not spend either.  ⚠⚠ Charging them drags `now − slip` backwards for as long as the guard stands, **its clock never flips, and it freezes at its first post — with every conformance count GREEN**, because a frozen rule agrees with a frozen body.  Measured: the mutation reads **112 000 000 units of slip, ONE anchor reached and a range of 4** where the true mover reads **0 / 2 / 4**, and § Rc's four equality gates saw none of it.  ⚠ So the discriminator is *am I standing on my own destination* — a carrier never can be, because the arrival after its last step already named the other anchor — and the general rule is worth more than the fix: ***conformance is an equality between two things that can stop together, so LIVENESS has to be asked separately***.  ⚠ It is `CLAUDE.md` § a gate that reads PERFECT is as suspect as one that reads wrong with a new shape: not a gate that cannot FAIL, but a gate whose two sides fail TOGETHER | 2026-08-28 | `plans/30`, `@M075`, `@X335`, `@M025` |

A mob breaking off its round to walk somewhere the round never goes is a FOURTH state and a SECOND exit, and `cycle_at` could not answer for it at all.  ⚠⚠ **So home is not somewhere a finished mob GOES — it is a hex the round already passes through, and the mob leaves the roster the tick its own cycle brings it there**: the mover, the cycle and the conformance gate are untouched, and the body is exactly where its rule says right up to the tick it is gone.  ⚠ **The bill is that two of the three shipped roles cannot END yet** — `haul` runs `work ↔ alt` and `guard` runs its post, so neither round touches home — and that is named rather than left open: `errand_row_fault` **refuses a shift on a row whose round cannot reach one**, so the column cannot promise behaviour the cycle has not got (`@X112`).  R5's POI is where a hauler's round gains a home leg.  ⚠⚠ **WHAT ENDS A ROUND IS A SPAN AND NOT A COUNT OF ROUNDS**: a count needs the CYCLE (two flow sweeps per mob per tick) while `now − slip` is already the only argument the rest of the file reads, and it **cannot oscillate** — `@FR-E-Bag-Steers` forbids a clock from choosing an ANCHOR (`@M073`), and a shift chooses nothing about direction, so a shift shorter than a round is simply *one round*.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE DEPARTURE HAD TO MOVE FROM THE CONSEQUENCE STAGE TO THE TOP OF THE NEXT TICK**, which is R4's claim rather than a tidy-up: taken at the consequence stage a robot arrives at its nest and is removed inside one tick, so **the last frame that ever holds it has it one hex short** — *deleted where it happened to stop*, wearing the new rule's clothes.  ⚠ And it is the same fact twice: what the player cannot see the GATE cannot see either, because the departure hex is unobservable from outside a tick that both moves and removes.  ⚠ Measured inert: `validate.sh` **50 scripts / 920 measurements**, unchanged | 2026-08-28 | `plans/30`, `@M076`, `@X112`, `@X335`, `@M073`, `@FR-E-Home-Is-A-Place` |
| `@X339` | ⚠⚠ **A HARVESTER'S DUMP AND ITS REPAIR SPOT ARE DIFFERENT PLACES — SO `haul` IS THE PLANT-MATERIAL GATHERER'S SHAPE AND `gather` IS THE NEST'S, AND `plans/30` R4 READ IT THE OTHER WAY ROUND.**  Owner, 2026-08-29: a plant-material gatherer *"has both a repair spot and a spot to dump the plants for processing as both fuel and plastics chemical extraction"*, and it is *"tied to the spots around the world that process this in the first pass and a distribution network that gets the first processed blocks to the places that need them in almost endless hunger"*.  ⚠⚠ **THREE PLACES, AND `Errand` ALREADY HAD ROOM FOR THEM**: `work` the picking ground, `alt` the **first-pass processing point** it dumps at every round, `home` the **repair point** it visits once in a while.  ⚠ R4 read the shipped `gather` row — *out empty, back laden to HOME* — as this robot, and it is not: that row is the shape where **the drop-off IS the home**, which is a nest or any node that both takes the load and services the carrier.  ⚠⚠ **AND A DISTRIBUTION ROBOT IS THE SAME ROW AGAIN** — out empty, back loaded — differing only in how far apart its anchors sit and what is in the bag, so the whole two-tier chain is `DESIGN.md` § 10's *one AI, per-class DATA* rather than three behaviours.  ⚠⚠ **The consequence is a NAMED GAP rather than a bug**: R4's ending needs `home` to be ON the round, because the closed form has to describe the mob right up to the tick it leaves (`@X338`) — a nest round touches home and a harvester round does not, so ***the ending does not reach the commonest robot in the world yet***.  What it needs is a **terminal leg** — two working legs repeating until the shift, then one walk home — which is a change to `cycle_build` / `cycle_phase`, and `errand_row_fault` already refuses a shift on a round that cannot reach home, so the gap is refused at construction rather than discovered as a robot that works for ever | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@X338`, `@X340`, `docs/SETTING.md`, `docs/ROBOT_ECONOMY.md`, `@FR-E-Home-Is-A-Place` |
| `@X340` | ⚠⚠ **THE ROBOT ECONOMY IS INPUT-CONSTRAINED AT EXACTLY TWO NODES — ORE AND CARBON — AND THE PLANET HAS NO FOSSIL CARBON AT ALL.**  Owner, 2026-08-29: *"this world lacks the gas/oil/coal layers of our world"*, and plant gathering is *"beside the gathering of raw metals, one of the biggest constraints on the robots of the planet"*.  ⚠⚠ **So every gram of fuel and of plastics feedstock starts as harvested growth**, which is why the colonisation programme has a terraforming half at all — `ROBOT_ECONOMY.md` § 6's *growth stands cultivated and harvested for carbon* is a colony that could not dig for it.  ⚠ Demand downstream is *"almost endless hunger"*, so the factories are not waiting for orders, they are waiting for material, and the flow **never lulls** — which is what lets `traffic_rate` be a steady per-minute figure rather than a schedule, and is `DESIGN.md` § What kind of game this is agreeing from the fiction's side.  ⚠⚠ **What it decides**: (1) ***denying a bottleneck is worth more than denying a surplus***, which gives `@X306`'s *rate the player DENIED* its teeth; (2) the swarm throws BODIES at a bottleneck, which is the arithmetic behind the harvester being the commonest machine in the world — *thin, slow, low-value* is the reading of ONE of them; (3) `@X317`'s *land in the overlap* gets its sharpest case, an ore route and a carbon route in reach of one base when there is time to sit on one; (4) it satisfies § The spreadsheet test, because neither input is purely good to interfere with — a carbon plant brings the insects the scrambler cannot deafen.  ⚠ **What it does NOT license**: an oil well, a refinery, a *found a fossil seam* discovery, a shortage EVENT, or a stock number on the HUD — a permanent constraint is not news, and the constraint is visible as TRAFFIC | 2026-08-29 | `@X339`, `@X306`, `@X317`, `@X298`, `docs/SETTING.md`, `docs/MATERIALS.md` |
| `@X341` | ⚠⚠ **A ROUND THAT ENDS AWAY FROM ITS ROUND IS A TERMINAL LEG, AND THE TURN IS A THIRD VALUE OF THE BAG.**  `plans/30` R4b, `src/errand.loft` § THE CYCLE + § THE MOVER.  ⚠ `@X339` left a NAMED GAP — R4's ending needs `home` ON the round, and the world's commonest robot dumps in one place and is serviced in another — and the shape that closes it is `alt → work → alt → … → alt → home`: the working legs repeat until the shift, then ONE walk to the repair point.  ⚠⚠ **It stays CLOSED-FORM because the turn point is arithmetic**: with `S = cycle_walked(rate, shift)` the mob turns at `T = ceil(S / period) × period`, so `cycle_phase` is *one modulo below `T`, one subtraction above it* and `@FR-E-Closed-Form` is untouched.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE BODY AGREES BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY BEING KEPT IN STEP** — which is the decision, not the arithmetic. So the turn is latched into `carry` as a third value, `ERRAND_BAG_HOMEWARD`, and `errand_leg` reads one number for all three legs.  ⚠ A second field saying *am I finished* would be a clock in all but name and would give `errand_leg` two things to read and a rule for when they disagree; the bag is where an ending belongs because **the bag steers** (`@FR-E-Bag-Steers`).  ⚠⚠ **The refusal moved from the ROW to the CYCLE**: `errand_row_fault` no longer asks for `home` on the round — it refuses only a CLOCK-steered row with a shift, whose phase is a time where a turn point is a distance — and `cycle_build` refuses the whole cycle when the walk home does not connect, exactly as it already refuses a working leg that does not.  ⚠ **`harvest` is a new catalogue row beside `haul` and not a change to it** (`@X322`, `@X329`): same two working legs, same walker, and the difference is DATA — a distribution robot on a long route is a round the player never sees end, a harvester's service trip is the ending this phase exists to give it.  ⚠ Measured inert: `validate.sh` 50 scripts / 920 measurements, unchanged | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@X339`, `@X340`, `@X338`, `@M077`, `@M078`, `@X322`, `@X329`, `@FR-E-Bag-Steers`, `@FR-E-Closed-Form`, `@FR-E-Home-Is-A-Place` |
| `@X342` | ⚠⚠ **THE BOUND'S CLAIM IS PER-LEG, AND THE UNION IS ONLY THE QUERY — A UNION IS SLACK, AND ASKING IT IS NOT ASKING ANYTHING.**  `plans/30` R5, `src/poi.loft` § THE BOUND, `tests/30_r5_the_bound.loft` (8).  ⚠ `@FR-E-Poi-Owns` states the bound as *the union over legs of the disc centred on each anchor with the incoming leg's length as its radius*, and the containment gate was written to match that sentence: **every hex the round reaches is inside the union**.  ⚠⚠ **It is very nearly unfalsifiable, and `@M080` measured it**: against the union, a radius **one hex short**, a **terminal leg given no disc at all** and a **rim excluded by an off-by-one** all read GREEN, and so did a sidestep that could INCREASE the distance — four of nine mutations, and the four load-bearing ones.  ⚠ The reason is geometry rather than a weak fixture: a hex outside its own leg's disc sits inside a neighbouring leg's, because consecutive discs are centred a leg apart and each has that leg's length as its radius.  ⚠⚠ **What `@FR-E-Non-Increasing` and `@X336` actually prove is about ONE LEG AT A TIME** — a body walking leg `i` is inside `disc(anchor_i, lengths_i)` — so the claim is `bound_disc_holds(b, leg, h)` and the union is a strictly larger promise nobody made.  ⚠⚠ **So there are two doors and the difference is NAMEABLE** (`@FR-F-Nameable-Difference`): the CLAIM asks a body about its own leg and a gate must ask it; the QUERY asks *could anything bounded by this be there*, where slack is exactly what is wanted, because over-answering costs work and under-answering is a defect.  ⚠ `bound_holds` is `bound_disc_holds` over every disc rather than a second copy of the comparison, so the two doors are one implementation.  ⚠⚠ **It generalises past this phase**: *a gate that asks a UNION cannot see a defect in a MEMBER*, which is `CLAUDE.md` § A gate aimed at the mechanism you expect to be the hazard with the quantifier as the subject | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@M079`, `@M080`, `@X336`, `@FR-E-Poi-Owns`, `@FR-E-Non-Increasing`, `@FR-E-Boundable` |
| `@X343` | ⚠⚠ **AN UN-MATERIALISED MOB IS A `slip` AND NOTHING ELSE — EVERYTHING THAT CAN BE A FUNCTION OF `t` IS ONE.**  `plans/30` R6a, `src/poi.loft` § WHAT AN UN-MATERIALISED MOB IS + § THE THREE TIERS, `tests/30_r6a_the_tiers.loft` (8).  ⚠ `@X299` needs a mob nobody can see to be **COMPUTED and not simulated coarsely**, and the question R6a had to answer is what such a mob must REMEMBER.  ⚠⚠ **The answer is one field.**  Which round it walks is an index; where on that round it started is its SEAT, and the offset is derived from it, so a rota of forty needs no forty authored phases; which hex it is on is `cycle_at`; **what is in its bag is derived from the leg** (`errand_bag_for`, `errand_leg`'s own map read backwards); and **what it is carrying toward its next hex is derived too** (`cycle_carry`).  ⚠⚠ **Only `slip` accumulates**, because only a BODY can be pushed — so a mob nobody has ever looked at needs no memory whatever, and one that has had a body must keep its lateness or `@FR-E-Slip` refunds the delay where nobody can watch it happen.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE BANK IS THE ONE NOBODY WOULD THINK OF** (`@M081`): a fresh body arrives with `bank_new()` while the rule is generally part-way through a hex, so a materialised mob releases its next hex LATE by exactly `cycle_carry` — perfect at the moment it appears, drifting from the tick after.  ⚠ **The three tiers are shaped by where the question is asked**: CULL is per-POI and is one `bound_meets` for a whole population (`@FR-E-Poi-Owns` cashed in), EVALUATE and MATERIALISE are per-mob.  ⚠⚠ **And a round ENDS on the mob's own clock, body or no body** — a population whose far half worked for ever while its near half retired would break R6b's equality and nobody could see it, since the far half is outside the window by definition | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@M081`, `@X299`, `@X342`, `@M014`, `@FR-E-Slip`, `@FR-E-Rule-Not-State`, `@FR-E-Place-State`, `@FR-E-Home-Is-A-Place` |
| `@X344` | ⚠⚠ **A POI'S REACH IS ONE INTEGER, AND THE CULL IN THE TICK IS ONE SUBTRACTION.**  `plans/30` R6b, `src/poi.loft` § THE REACH + § THE STEP, `src/play.loft::play_one_tick`, `tests/30_r6b_the_materialiser.loft` (8).  ⚠ R5's bound is a union of discs and R6a's `poi_survey` asks it with `bound_meets` — but the bound needs the rounds, and the rounds are two flow sweeps a route, so **a POI culled every tick for a whole sortie would pay for the rounds that prove it can be**.  ⚠⚠ **So the bound is folded ONCE to a single radius about the POI's own hex**: `max(lat_distance(poi, centre_i) + radius_i)`, which contains the union and makes the tick's per-POI question `lat_distance(poi, player) <= reach + window` — no world read, no round built.  ⚠ A superset again, the same safe direction `bound_holds` takes (`@X342`): over-answering costs work, under-answering is a defect, and **an UNDER-estimate is exactly what `max(radius)` would be** — invisible on a straight out-and-back, where the furthest a mob gets from its place is also the largest disc, and a defect on every three-anchor round and every bend.  ⚠⚠ **The cache is a `vector<integer>` and it lives on `PlayState`, not on `WaveState`** — DERIVED rather than authored, which is the division `state_diff` already draws; | `@X346` | ⚠⚠ **A HEX WALKED AWAY FROM ITS ANCHOR COSTS A MOB TWO, AND THAT IS WHAT LETS ONE LEAVE ITS ROUTE AT ALL.**  `plans/30` R7b, `src/errand.loft` § THE LURE, `errand_hex_cost` / `errand_lure_at` / `errand_lure_take` / `errand_lure_drop`, `tests/30_r7b_the_distraction.loft` (7), `tests/scripts/a-heap-a-hauler-took.keys` + `a-heap-a-gatherer-walked-past.keys`.  ⚠ `@FR-E-Built-Not-Seen` wants a mob DRAWN OFF its route by something the player left, and `@FR-E-Slip` says a deviation costs TIME and never DESTINATION — *a body pushed off its cycle re-converges on the same hex*.  ⚠⚠ **A DETOUR HAS TO BE PAID FOR BOTH WAYS AND THE RETURN LEG IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ORDINARY PROGRESS**: a mover charging one hex per hex spent going out leaves the body permanently short of its own rule by the depth of the detour, silently and for ever.  ⚠ So the charge is read from the ANCHOR's distance before and after — **0 closer, 1 equal, 2 further** — and then with `i` steps out and `d` back the body stands at `D + i − d`, the rule has advanced `(i + d) − 2i = d − i`, and `D − (d − i)` is the same number: the two agree in DISTANCE at every moment of the detour with nothing remembered.  ⚠⚠ **`@FR-E-Place-State` is what makes that the required answer rather than a clever one** — a mob may hold nothing beyond `carry` and `slip`, so a debt COUNTER is not available and the debt is paid at the moment it is incurred.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE BAG IS NOT TOUCHED BY A THEFT**: the bag is the ROUND's state and `cycle_phase` reads the WALK, so a pickup that flipped it would put the body on a different leg from its own rule. | `@M047` | ⚠⚠ **THE BLOCKER WAS STALE, AND THE REAL HAZARD WAS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ONE DOCUMENTED.**  BACKLOG B1.  `@X097`/`@X130` said `draw_text` is unreachable under `loft test`; probed on the pinned `graphics` 0.5.2, interpreted, it draws — `gl_load_font` → handle **0**, `gl_measure_text("WALLET", 16)` → **63.60 px**, `gl_text_height` → **19**, and one 18 px line lights **1324** pixels.  Controls: content moves it (`'42'`=128, `'888'`=243), size moves it (`'42'@32`=399), the empty string draws **0**.  ⚠⚠ **The trap: a font that FAILED to load draws in whatever font loaded first.**  With a good font at handle 0, a failed `gl_load_font` returns null and `gl_measure_text(null,…)` answers **12.34** — identical to the good handle — while `draw_text` lays down the same **128** pixels; with NO font ever loaded it draws 0, which is exactly what makes the trap invisible in a one-font test.  ⚠⚠ **The two defences stop DIFFERENT failures, and the falsification is what said so**: deleting `text_draw`'s `font_ready` guard leaves a *missing file* drawing nothing (`FONT_NO_HANDLE` = -1 is not a handle `graphics` uses) and only corrupts the ANSWER (`drew=true`, `lit=0`), while a `Font` carrying a **live** handle with `loaded` false paints the good font exactly — **203 pixels against 203**.  ⚠⚠ **Which is also why `loaded` is a second field over one fact**, against `carry.loft`'s own rule: under [loft#914] a partial literal takes the silent default, and measured, omitting `loaded` gives `ready=false` (**fails closed**) where readiness-as-`handle >= 0` would omit `handle` → **0** → a live handle (**fails into the trap**).  ⚠ Path: `{source_dir()}/../assets/…` resolves from BOTH entry shapes — `tests/` and `src/` — and `#cwd` does **not** move `source_dir()`.  ⚠⚠ **So ABSOLUTE is ENFORCED at the door rather than documented**, and the hazard is live rather than theoretical: `is_file("assets/DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf")` answers **true** from the repo root, so a relative path passes every check dryopea could make and then means something else to `gl_load_font` depending on the release installed.  ⚠⚠ **What text COSTS a classified frame, for the first consumer**: of 1324 lit pixels only **550 are pure white** and **774 are blended**, so `classify_canvas`'s exact lookup puts 58% of every glyph in `unknown` — `@X077`'s `other == 0` and `@X092`'s `unknown - entity == 0` are both assertions text drawn into a classified frame breaks.
