| `@M081` | ⚠⚠ **TEN MUTATIONS, NINE CAUGHT, AND BOTH SURVIVORS WERE FAULTS IN THE GATE RATHER THAN GAPS IN IT.**  `plans/30` R6a, `tests/30_r6a_the_tiers.loft` (8).  ⚠ Caught: a fresh body handed an EMPTY bank ✅ (7 of 24 ticks disagree), releasing forgetting `slip` ✅, materialising forgetting the seat offset ✅, the body handed an empty BAG ✅, the cull tier asking anyway ✅, a seat spreading nothing ✅, a computed round never ending ✅, and `cycle_carry` dropping its remainder ✅.  ⚠⚠ **SURVIVOR 1 WAS A GATE COMPARING A FUNCTION WITH ITSELF**: the round cache indexed by POI instead of by route read green, because the test compared `poi_bound_from` against `poi_bound` — and `poi_bound` IS `poi_bound_from` over `poi_cycles`.  **A wrapper is a golden of its own delegate** (`plans/09`'s rule with a new subject: a golden rebaselined during the change under test has verified nothing), and the fix was an independent ORACLE in the test that builds its own rounds.  ⚠⚠ **SURVIVOR 2 IS A REDUNDANCY WITH A NAME**: `errand_bag_for` asks `row.laden` before the terminal leg, and swapping them changes nothing because `errand_terminal` already refuses a row whose `laden` is `ANCHOR_HOME` — the two conditions cannot both hold.  ⚠ **And the mover the fixture walks is a SCOUT because a REGULAR cannot see the bank at all** (`@M014`'s class, a fifth instance in this plan, asserted rather than assumed): at 1.5 hex/s a hex is exactly one tick, so a body materialised on a tick boundary carries ZERO on 24 of 24 ticks and an implementation that threw the carry away is green for it at every speed of release | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@X343`, `@M014`, `@M080`, `@X336` |
| `@M082` | ⚠⚠ **ELEVEN MUTATIONS, SEVEN CAUGHT, AND ALL FOUR SURVIVORS WERE THINGS THE GATE COULD NOT SEE RATHER THAN THINGS THE CODE GOT RIGHT.**  `plans/30` R6b, `tests/30_r6b_the_materialiser.loft` (8).  ⚠⚠ **SURVIVOR 1 — A SAVING IS NOT A BEHAVIOUR.**  Deleting the cull entirely changed no position and made no extra body, because a mob outside the window gets no body either way: **the cull is work NOT DONE, and only a COUNT can see it** (`@M029` again, from the other side).  `poi_step` grew a fourth answer — mobs looked at — and the far run reads **0 against 160**.  ⚠⚠ **SURVIVOR 2 — THE FIXTURE'S GEOMETRY HID THE FORMULA.**  `poi_reach` taking the largest RADIUS instead of the largest DISTANCE PLUS radius is exactly right on a straight out-and-back, where the furthest a mob gets from its place IS one leg; it is a defect on a three-anchor round or a bend, and no played fixture on that shape can tell them apart — so the claim is asserted DIRECTLY, on two discs 10 apart.  ⚠⚠ **SURVIVOR 3 — A CORPSE IS STILL ON THE ROSTER.**  A killed body is MARKED and not removed (bodies are terrain), so the record's sweep found it for ever and never learned the player had killed it; the fix is one `&& alive` and the gate is a mob killed mid-run that must not be made again.  ⚠ **SURVIVOR 4 — two fields moving together**: `poi_state_set` writes `state` and `since` at once, so a gate that changed both could not say which one it was reading — the fix is the same `since` in both worlds and a different state | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@X344`, `@M081`, `@M029` |
| `@M083` | ⚠⚠ **TWELVE MUTATIONS, TWELVE CAUGHT — AND THE TWO DEFECTS THE PHASE ACTUALLY FOUND WERE FOUND BY TRIPWIRES INSTEAD.**  `plans/30` R7a, `tests/30_r7a_the_places_said.loft` (9).  ⚠ The sweep: seats starting at 1, a population one short, a `mob` line written unconditionally, a `poi` line without its `since`, a `routine` without its back-pointer, a `route` that skips `poi_route_fault`, an unknown state defaulting to `working`, an unknown bag defaulting to `empty`, the schema's third pair dropped, `routine` emitted only for a robot with a ROLE, `poi_state_set` forgetting `since`, and the bag rows swapped under their constants.  ⚠⚠ **But a green mutation sweep is a statement about the gate and not about the tree, and both real defects arrived from somewhere else**: `compare.loft`'s `errand` row — laid the same hour — went red on `a-road-that-passes-by` because the flag and the role are two facts (`@X345`), and `tests/18_s2`'s corpus sweep went red on the first scenario with a POI because `slip` is the one duration that may be NEGATIVE (`@D009`).  ⚠ **Neither was in the mutation list, because a mutation list is written by the person who wrote the code.**  That is `@M081`'s lesson from the other side: there, both survivors were faults in the gate; here, both findings were outside it.  ⚠⚠ **AND A THIRD CAME FROM R1's OWN SWEEP**: `test_no_role_is_ever_compared_anywhere_in_src` named both new verbs refusing an unknown role with `== ROLE_NONE`, which is `@X333` exactly.  ⚠ The cause is that `errand_role_named` **cannot say no** — it answers the zero row for a word it does not know and `none` is a legal word, so *a typo* and *the role with no round* come back one number.  The fix is `errand_role_known` (*the row I got back is called what I asked for*) and `poi_kind_known` beside it, because the kind table had the same hole | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@X345`, `@D009`, `@M081` |
| `@M084` | ⚠⚠ **THIRTY POINTS OF A TWO-HUNDRED-POINT WALLET, AND THE PAIR IS ONE TABLE COLUMN APART.**  `plans/30` R7b.  `a-heap-a-gatherer-walked-past.keys` reads **230.0** and `a-heap-a-hauler-took.keys` reads **200.0** over the same road, the same side track, the same 1.5 m heap and the same ticks — a `gather` where the other runs a `haul`, which is the one column `errand_roles()` differs in.  ***It steals your income* is a number rather than a picture.**  ⚠⚠ **AND THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS IN THE SAME FILE AND IT IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS**: the player parks one hex off the road, in plain sight, for THIRTY TICKS, and the robot is on its rule's own hex to the tick — `enemy 0 36 0`, which the closed form gives for offset 30 of a 32-hex round.  That is `crawler`'s defect assert-able: without its *an incursion does not break formation for a hero it has merely seen* rule, raiders parked on a hero for seven days and the mechanism *"silently became 'monsters walk at the player'"*.  ⚠⚠ **Thirteen mutations, twelve caught, and the survivor was the load's MATERIAL**: a spill that lost `errand_load_source` gives the player back the wrong rubble — possibly uncollectable SPOIL, which is their salvage destroyed rather than returned — and every count stayed green because *a robot's corpse is wreckage too*, so a fixture that used wreckage for the heap could not tell the load from the carrier.  ⚠ The heap is MASONRY now, and the spill is measured as a DIFFERENCE against a thief carrying nothing (2.0 m against 0.5) rather than as a total | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@X346`, `@M073`, `@M083` |
| `@M085` | ⚠⚠ **A ROBOT ROAD IS WORTH 146 TICKS OF A BASE'S LIFE — AND NOTHING AT ALL TO A BASE THE WAVE HAS ALREADY OVERWHELMED.**  `plans/30` R8, `tests/scripts/a-base-on-a-robot-road.keys` + `a-base-beside-a-robot-road.keys` — the same map, wall, waves and painted road, differing in the four coordinates that put the round inside the scrambler bubble or 36 hexes out of it.  ⚠ **123 ticks against 269**, and seven enemies alive against three: four haulers nobody sent at the player lose their link crossing the bubble (`wave_cutoff`, one way) and join the siege on the same front.  ⚠⚠ **AND THE SWEEP IS THE FINDING**: the road is worth **196** ticks against a wave of two, **146** against three, **10** against five and **0** against eight.  The siege front is the WALL'S WIDTH (`@M020`), so a wave of eight already saturates it and four more bodies change nothing — **the first version of this pair ran the authored 5 + 8 and read 118 against 118 with four extra besiegers plainly on the map.**  That is `CLAUDE.md` § *a gate whose reading is already saturated cannot see what you built* met by pricing the SUPPLY against the CAPACITY rather than by believing a flat reading.  ⚠ It answers three questions at once: `@X305`'s *a POI earns its place only if REMOVING it moves the clock*, `@X317`'s *land in the overlap* priced from the player's side, and `@X303`'s *does this make behaviour more BELIEVABLE, or does it only simulate MORE?* — which `plans/30` § R8 says a phase that adds ticks of work and moves no clock has answered the wrong way.  ⚠⚠ **And the pair found a THIRD independence**: `wave_cutoff` clears the errand FLAG and LEAVES THE ANCHORS, so a hauler the bubble took is `ROLE_HAUL` with the flag cleared — the mirror of R7a's ambient robot, which is `ROLE_NONE` with the flag SET.  `emit_enemies` keyed on the flag alone and dropped the whole routine, and `tests/18_s2`'s corpus sweep named it the first time this pair ran.  The `routine` verb gained an `<on|cut>` token | 2026-08-29 | `plans/30`, `@M020`, `@X303`, `@X305`, `@X317` |
| `@M086` | ⚠⚠ **A SORTIE'S CARRY BUYS FOUR BEACONS WHERE THE FIRST BASE BOUGHT TWO — THE LANDING EXCLUSION MOVES WITH THE WALLET.**  `plans/31` N5, `tests/scripts/a-landing-a-sortie-paid-for.keys` against `the-opening-two-hundred.keys`.  ⚠ `@M065` measured *exactly TWO, and the third press is refused* and read it as `@X288`'s landing exclusion arrived at by ARITHMETIC rather than by a rule.  ⚠⚠ **That measurement is about a FIRST base.**  A sortie that carried 200 out opens the next at 400, `beacon_buy` reads `wallet_left` and nothing else, and the same arithmetic gives **four beacons and a fifth press refused** — so the landing loadout is not a menu a good run unlocks, it is the same rule with a different first term.  ⚠ And it still costs a TRIP each: four presses at the core and four drives out, the fourth as long as the first — so what a sortie earns is **more to do**, not less, which is `DESIGN.md` § What kind of game this is applied to its own economy | 2026-08-29 | `plans/31`, `@X347`, `@M065`, `@X288`, `@M068` |

---

## How to use this

```bash
grep -rn '@X025' .          # everywhere the permit decision is cited
grep -rn '@M001' .          # everywhere the 75% figure is quoted
grep -rn '@X0[0-9][0-9]' docs/ | wc -l
```

⚠ **Adding a row is cheap and removing one is not.**  A code is permanent even
after the decision is reversed — a reversed decision keeps its code and gains a
`⚠ SUPERSEDED by @X###` line, exactly as `PROBLEMS.md` keeps `@D001` after the
fix.  That is what makes an old commit message still resolvable.

⚠ **This index does not cover the older docs yet** — `DESIGN.md`, `SETTING.md`,
`ENEMY_MOVEMENT.md`, `ROBOT_ECONOMY.md` and `GROUND_TYPES.md` carry many
decisions with no codes.  Backfilling them is worth doing when something needs
to cite one, not as a sweep: a code nobody references is a code nobody
maintains.
